## How to read this document

Findings are ranked by real-world blast radius on a live deployment, not by code aesthetics.

| Severity | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Critical** | Cross-tenant data exposure, auth bypass, irreversible data loss/corruption, or secret compromise. Act before next deploy. |
| **High** | Production breakage, stuck/undeletable state, or a security gap needing a specific precondition. Act this cycle. |
| **Medium** | Degraded reliability, unbounded growth, or a gap needing an unlikely precondition. Schedule. |
| **Low** | Hygiene and latent risk. Backlog. |

**Evidence rule:** every finding carries `file:line` and a concrete failure scenario. Findings that could not be verified are explicitly labelled *(unverified hypothesis)*.

**ADR discipline:** risks already documented as deliberate trade-offs are NOT listed as findings. They appear under *ADR-Accepted Risks — Re-confirm or Revoke* so the severity list stays honest.

**Definition of Done (added 2026-10-01).** This audit is complete when all four of the following hold; they are the criteria against which its closure is judged.

1. **Every backlog finding is traced and adjudicated.** Each finding carries a final status — OPEN, CLOSED/RESOLVED, or DEFERRED — assigned by a dated adjudication block rather than left at a phase-time or superseded value.
2. **Every open question is resolved or formally deferred.** No Open Question remains as free-text "outstanding": it is either answered in place or converted to a **DEFERRED — ACCESS-BLOCKED** entry that names its blocking condition, the finding whose severity it bounds, and the exact access needed to close it.
3. **No contradictory verdicts remain.** A finding's own entry and every reconciliation table, pointer or status line that mentions it agree on the same status; where an older wording is retained for traceability it is explicitly marked SUPERSEDED.
4. **No finding is left without an owner.** Every finding, and every adjudicated row of the claim-verification table, names the layer or explicit owner responsible for the fix.

The audit is **complete with deferrals** when these four hold. Deferrals bound the affected fixes; they do not bound the audit's closure.

## Findings

### Layer 1 — Trust & Tenant Boundary

#### Phase 1.1 — Session & Credential Lifecycle

Phase verdict: **no Critical defects.** The session core is sound; all defects are at the lifecycle edges. 3 High, 4 Medium, 3 Low.

##### High

**H-1. Sign-in rate limiter bypassable by varying the username — unauthenticated Argon2 work amplification (DoS)**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/auth/rate-limiter.ts:14-18` (`buildRateLimitKey` returns `${cleanIp}:${cleanUser}`); `apps/backend/src/modules/auth/auth-service.ts:102` keys the limiter on that pair; `auth-service.ts:117-121` runs a full dummy Argon2id verify before recording failure. Each distinct username creates a new counter row, so no lockout is ever reached.
- Failure scenario: Unauthenticated loop against `POST /api/v1/auth/sign-in` with an incrementing username (`probe1`, `probe2`, …). Every request triggers `argon2.verify` at 64 MiB / 3 iterations (`adapters/crypto/argon2.ts:3-8`), an accounts SELECT, a rate-limit upsert, and an audit INSERT. Single backend container exhausts CPU/memory; rate-limit and audit tables grow unbounded; citizen-facing service goes down. The same bypass removes any real brute-force ceiling for a known username across rotating source IPs.
- Proposed fix: Add an account-independent limiter keyed on IP alone (plus a coarse global counter), checked **before** the dummy-hash path, returning 429 with no Argon2 work performed.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**H-2. Origin guard treats `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-site` as trusted — CSRF from any sibling subdomain**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/auth/origin-guard.ts:65-67` returns early for both `same-origin` and `same-site`, before the Origin comparison at lines 71-88.
- Failure scenario: `same-site` means same registrable domain, not same origin. The session cookie is `SameSite=Strict` (`auth-routes.ts:56`), also registrable-domain scoped, so a browser on `anything.mahalla-ovozi.uz` sends the session cookie to `mahalla-ovozi.uz` and the guard returns early. An attacker controlling a sibling subdomain can issue body-less state-changing requests without CORS preflight — e.g. `POST /api/v1/districts/:districtId/hokim-account/disable` or `/replace` (`modules/hokim-accounts/hokim-accounts-routes.ts:99,118`) — locking a district's Hokim out. Preflighted JSON-body routes remain protected by CORS.
- Proposed fix: Never return early on `same-site`. Only `same-origin` may skip the Origin comparison; `same-site` must fall through to the `isOriginAllowed` check against `APP_ORIGIN`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**H-3. DISTRICT_HOKIM sessions have no absolute expiry — a stolen token can be kept alive indefinitely**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/auth/session-manager.ts:118-131` — when `sessionAgeMs >= ABSOLUTE_TIMEOUT_MS` and role is `DISTRICT_HOKIM`, `effectiveCreatedAt = now` is written back to the row (line 149), restarting the 24 h ceiling on every touch.
- Failure scenario: An attacker holding a Hokim session token (kiosk/wall display, copied browser profile, proxy log) issues one request every <12 h. Both idle and absolute windows renew forever, and the session survives district suspension/restore. This is the role that can read a district's citizen data.
- Proposed fix: Store a separate `absoluteExpiresAt` written once at creation and never extended, or cap renewal (e.g. only renew while `now - originalCreatedAt < 30 days`).
- Confidence: Confirmed (behaviour is intentional per the in-code comment; the unbounded consequence appears unintended — see Open Question OQ-1)

##### Medium

**M-1. `secure` cookie flag derived from `req.protocol` with no boot-time assertion; cookie lacks `__Host-` prefix**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/auth/auth-routes.ts:52-58` (`secure: req.protocol === 'https'`); `entrypoints/http.ts:100` (`trustProxy: true`); `deploy/compose/Caddyfile:19-20` sets `X-Forwarded-Proto`. Cookie is named `mahalla_session` (`session-manager.ts:9`), though the comment at `auth-routes.ts:51` claims a `__Host-` rationale.
- Failure scenario: Correct only while Caddy forwards `X-Forwarded-Proto`. If the proxy config regresses (header dropped, extra proxy, direct container access), `req.protocol` is `http` and the session cookie is issued without `Secure`, exposing the raw token to plaintext transport — with no startup failure to signal it. Non-`__Host-` name also lets a sibling subdomain overwrite `mahalla_session` for the parent domain (forced-logout DoS; not hijack, since tokens are validated server-side).
- Proposed fix: Fail fast at boot in production unless `APP_ORIGIN` is `https://`; set `secure: true` unconditionally in production. Rename to a `__Host-` prefixed cookie once TLS-only serving is guaranteed.
- Confidence: Confirmed (failure is a misconfiguration dependency; current Caddy containment verified)

**M-2. Sliding expiry is never re-issued to the browser — cookie dies 12 h after sign-in regardless of activity**
- Evidence: `auth-routes.ts:52-58` is the only `setCookie` call in the codebase (verified by grep); `session-manager.ts:46` sets initial `expiresAt = now + IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`; `session-manager.ts:145-154` updates the DB row only.
- Failure scenario: A PRODUCT_OWNER active at hour 11 is logged out at hour 12 because the browser drops the cookie at its original expiry, even though the server row slid forward. The 24 h absolute ceiling is unreachable in practice; the Hokim kiosk needs a fresh sign-in every 12 h.
- Proposed fix: Re-issue `Set-Cookie` with the refreshed `expiresAt` on each touch update, or use a session cookie with no `expires` and rely solely on server-side expiry.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**M-3. District suspension does not revoke sessions server-side (contradicts ADR 0007)**
- Evidence: `modules/subscriptions/subscriptions-service.ts:619-625` sets `districts.status = 'SUSPENDED'` with no session mutation; access is blocked at request time in `require-auth.ts:92-139`. `docs/adr/0007-stateful-sessions-over-jwt.md:18` claims suspension "immediately purges or invalidates active sessions in the database".
- Failure scenario: A suspended district's Hokim sessions remain valid rows and continue to slide (`session-manager.ts:110-154` performs no district status check). They resume working immediately on restore. `/api/v1/auth/session` (`auth-routes.ts:93-117`) is not behind `require-auth` and keeps returning actor and expiry data for a suspended district. The guard-layer block is effective for district-scoped data, so this is an assurance/lifecycle gap, not a data leak.
- Proposed fix: Call `revokeAllAccountSessions` for the district's accounts inside the same suspension transaction, and/or add a district-status check to `validateAndTouchSession`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**M-4. No account-scoped or global-IP lockout; lockout does not extend under sustained attack**
- Evidence: `rate-limiter.ts:92-100` — `lockedUntil` is written only when `isNowLocked && !result.lockedUntil`, and the upsert `set` clause (lines 62-85) never updates `lockedUntil`. Limiter is called only from `auth-service.ts:105,121,134,150` (verified by grep).
- Failure scenario: Because the key is `(ip, username)`, an attacker rotating source addresses gets 5 attempts per pair per 15 min forever; no account-wide counter exists, so a targeted account is never locked globally. Practical impact is bounded by the ≥15-character password policy (`password-policy.ts:21`) and 108-bit generated temporary passwords.
- Proposed fix: Add an account-only limiter key (`buildRateLimitKey('*', username)`) plus a coarse per-IP limiter, and refresh `lockedUntil` while attempts continue during an active lockout.
- Confidence: Confirmed

##### Low

**L-1. No garbage collection for session or rate-limit rows** — `session-manager.ts` has no `delete(sessions)`; only district-deletion, `retention/restore-reconciliation.ts:347`, and `cli/clean-test-data.ts:55` delete sessions. `rate-limiter.ts:108-110` deletes only on successful sign-in. Rows accumulate for the deployment's lifetime, growing tables and indexes. No security impact today. Fix: scheduled purge of revoked/expired sessions and stale rate-limit rows. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-2. Password policy has no entropy or composition floor; no Argon2 rehash-on-login** — `password-policy.ts:19-44` enforces only code-point length (15–128) plus an exact-match blocklist; `argon2.ts:14-19` never inspects `argon2.needsRehash`. A long low-entropy password (keyboard walk) is accepted, and raising Argon2 cost later will not upgrade existing hashes. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-3. Hardcoded development encryption key present in source** — `adapters/crypto/token-cipher.ts:17` (`FALLBACK_DEV_KEY`), guarded at line 30 by a `NODE_ENV === 'production'` throw. No production impact as written. **PROMOTED to Critical as C-1 by Phase 1.4**, which established that no boot-time assertion exists, so the fallback is reachable by any process started without `NODE_ENV=production`. See C-1.

##### Verified sound

- Session token generation: `crypto.randomBytes(32)` → base64url, 256-bit CSPRNG (`session-manager.ts:11-14`); DB stores only SHA-256, never the raw token (`session-manager.ts:16-18,43,48-57`).
- Session fixation: sign-in always inserts a new session row with a fresh token (`session-manager.ts:42-57`); no pre-auth session is upgraded in place; roles never change in place, so no privilege-change rotation is needed.
- Logout genuinely revokes the server row (`auth-routes.ts:82-89` → `revokeSessionByToken`, `session-manager.ts:168-175`).
- Revocation on credential change is transactional and complete: PO reset (`account-service.ts:52-75`), Hokim reset/disable/replace (`hokim-accounts-service.ts:283-319,356-390,434-459`), first-login change (`auth-service.ts:288-336`) all bump `credentialVersion` and call `revokeAllAccountSessions` in the same transaction; `validateAndTouchSession` revokes on version mismatch and on non-ACTIVE status (`session-manager.ts:98-108`).
- `revokeAllAccountSessions` preserves the caller's own session via `ne(sessions.id, exceptSessionId)` (`session-manager.ts:182-195`).
- Absolute (24 h) and idle (12 h) expiry enforced server-side from DB timestamps on every request, never trusted from the cookie (`session-manager.ts:110-138`).
- Argon2id parameters strong, salt handling correct: memoryCost 65536 KiB, timeCost 3, parallelism 1, argon2id, library-managed per-hash random salt (`argon2.ts:3-11`). No plaintext or reversibly-encrypted password storage.
- User-enumeration timing equalised: unknown accounts run a real Argon2 verify against a module-load dummy hash before the same 401 (`auth-service.ts:62,117-128`); status branches only reached after successful verification.
- Rate-limiter state is PostgreSQL-persisted and cross-process, not in-memory (`rate-limits.ts:3-18`); counter update is a single atomic `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` (`rate-limiter.ts:50-86`), eliminating read-modify-write races.
- Origin guard fails closed on missing `Origin` when `Sec-Fetch-Site` is absent/inconclusive (`origin-guard.ts:8,90-99`). (Scope note: Phase 1.2 established the guard is attached at the **root** instance, so it applies app-wide — see the Phase 1.1 pointer retraction under Cross-Layer Pointers.)
- Webhook origin-guard exemption is not a gap: `/api/v1/telegram/webhook/:botId` and `/api/v1/webhooks/telegram/:botId` verify an HMAC-derived secret with `crypto.timingSafeEqual` over fixed-length SHA-256 digests (`webhook-security.ts:8-38`, `telegram-intake-routes.ts:38-45`).
- Cookie flags `httpOnly: true`, `sameSite: 'strict'`, `path: '/'` present on every `setCookie`/`clearCookie` call (`auth-routes.ts:52-58,88,101,128`; `require-auth.ts:39-44`). No cookie or credential value written to audit metadata (asserted `tests/auth-lifecycle.test.ts:320-331`).
- `X-Forwarded-For` is **overwritten** (not appended) by Caddy with `{remote_host}` (`Caddyfile:19`), so `req.ip` and the rate-limit key are not client-spoofable through the deployed proxy; the backend container publishes no host ports in production (`docker-compose.prod.yml:20-53`).
- TLS terminated at Caddy with HSTS `max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload` (`Caddyfile:7`); plain-HTTP VPS-IP access redirects to the canonical domain (`Caddyfile:43-45`).

#### Phase 1.2 — Authorization Matrix (route × role)

Phase verdict: **no Critical, no High.** All **87 registered routes** across 16 route files are guarded, public-by-design (health liveness), or HMAC-verified. No cross-tenant or unauthenticated state-changing path found. No role-escalation path exists. 2 Medium, 5 Low.

##### Medium

**M-5. App-wide origin guard depends on an accidental registration-order side effect**
- Evidence: `modules/auth/auth-routes.ts:20` attaches `verifyStateChangingOrigin` to the **root** Fastify instance only because `registerAuthRoutes` happens to be the first call in `entrypoints/http.ts:57`. Every other scope inherits it implicitly via Fastify's parent-hook copying (`plugin-override.js:38-47`, `hooks.js:73-91`, `fastify.js:618-620`).
- Failure scenario: A future refactor that wraps `registerAuthRoutes` inside `fastify.register(async (scope) => ...)`, or reorders it after another module, **silently removes CSRF/origin protection from the entire application** with no test-failure signal in any file that does not assert `FORBIDDEN_ORIGIN`. A malicious site could then drive state-changing PO actions (e.g. `POST /api/v1/districts/:districtId/subscription/execute-live-deletion`) using a victim PO's cookie. **This is the load-bearing consequence of Phase 1.1's H-2: because the guard is global, H-2's `same-site` early-return weakens CSRF defence on all 87 routes, not a subset.**
- Proposed fix: Move `server.addHook('preHandler', verifyStateChangingOrigin)` into `buildHttpServer` (`entrypoints/http.ts`, beside the CORS registration ~line 136) and delete it from `auth-routes.ts:20`, making the global guard explicit and order-independent.
- Confidence: Confirmed (Fastify framework source read; not executed — read-only phase)

**M-6. `/api/v1/auth/*` hand-rolled session checks bypass the district-status gate that `createRequireAuth` applies**
- Evidence: `modules/auth/auth-routes.ts:99,126` call `validateAndTouchSession` directly instead of `createRequireAuth`. The district `accessEligible`/status gate (SUSPENDED / CANCELLED / SETUP_INCOMPLETE) lives **only** in `require-auth.ts:81-140`.
- Failure scenario: A DISTRICT_HOKIM whose district was later SUSPENDED or CANCELLED can still call `POST /api/v1/auth/change-first-login-password` and mutate their credential/`credentialVersion`, and still gets a 200 from `GET /api/v1/auth/session` reporting a valid actor. A suspended district's operator retains a usable, refreshed session identity. Compounds with H-3 (Hokim sessions never absolutely expire) and M-3 (suspension does not revoke sessions) — together these three mean **suspension is not a reliable cut-off** for a district operator.
- Proposed fix: Extract the district-status block from `require-auth.ts:81-140` into a shared `assertDistrictServiceable(db, account)` helper and call it from both `/api/v1/auth/session` and `/api/v1/auth/change-first-login-password`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

##### Low

**L-4. `requireDistrictId` option is dead code** — `require-auth.ts:10` declares `requireDistrictId?: boolean` and `:191` passes it, but the body never reads it. No exposure today (`require-auth.ts:81-90` independently rejects a DISTRICT_HOKIM with no `districtId`, and `schema/accounts.ts:29-31` enforces the same via CHECK). It is a trap for a maintainer who trusts the flag. Fix: delete the option or implement it and drop the duplicate check. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-5. `allowMatchingDistrictOnly` silently no-ops unless the district is a URL param** — `require-auth.ts:144` reads `req.params?.districtId` only; never query or body. No live exposure: the sole consumer is `createRequireDistrictAccess` at `ai-operations-routes.ts:71` on `/api/v1/districts/:districtId/ai-operations`, which is param-based. But reusing this guard on a body-scoped route would pass the tenant check unconditionally for any DISTRICT_HOKIM. Fix: accept an extractor (`getDistrictId: (req) => ...`) or assert at construction that the route path contains `:districtId`. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-6. Unauthenticated `/api/v1/health/ready` discloses internal topology** — `modules/health/health-routes.ts:164-205` is registered on the root with no guard and returns `checks.database`, `checks.queue`, `checks.restoreReconciliation`. An unauthenticated internet caller learns DB/queue availability and, via `restoreReconciliation: 'unreconciled'`, whether a disaster-restore reconciliation is pending — reconnaissance against a degraded live system holding citizen data. Fix: keep `/health/live` public; gate `/health/ready` behind a shared-secret header or loopback/proxy restriction, returning only a boolean publicly. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-7. Redundant double execution of the origin guard** — ten scopes re-register `verifyStateChangingOrigin` on top of the inherited root hook: `districts-routes.ts:31`, `hokim-accounts-routes.ts:27`, `issues-routes.ts:49`, `health-routes.ts:245`, `userbot-session-routes.ts:111`, `telegram-bot-routes.ts:23`, `subscriptions-routes.ts:72`, `telegram-groups-routes.ts:142`, `district-topics-routes.ts:31`, `hokim-topics-routes.ts:70`. No security impact (idempotent); wastes work and obscures which layer enforces. Fix: after M-5 makes the root hook explicit, delete all per-scope registrations. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-8. `req.actor` field access in userbot routes reads non-existent property names** — `userbot-session-routes.ts:135-136,179-180` read `actorId`/`actorRole`, which are not members of `ActorContext` (`packages/api-contracts/src/auth.ts:6-13`); the `?? req.actor.id` / `?? req.actor.role` fallback always wins. No live impact (fallback is correct). Dead expression that hides intent. Fix: use `req.actor.id` / `req.actor.role` directly. Confidence: Confirmed

##### Verified sound (Phase 1.2)

- Every guard derives role/district/account from the **server-side session row** (`require-auth.ts:54,162-180`); no guard trusts a client-supplied role, district id, or account id.
- `require-auth.ts` returns 401 for missing/invalid session and 403 for role, district-activity, and tenant-mismatch failures — correct status semantics.
- Guards are registered as `preHandler`, which Fastify runs before the handler (`handle-request.js:150-159`); a guard that replies causes `preHandlerCallback` to return early (`:163`) and `hookIterator` to skip remaining hooks (`hooks.js:410-412`). **No handler-after-guard ordering bug exists.**
- No guard has an optional-auth fast path; `require-auth.ts:26-35` fails closed on a missing cookie.
- Both Telegram webhook ingresses are HMAC-verified with constant-time compare (`webhook-security.ts:22-38`) — legitimate origin-guard exemptions.
- `POST .../simulate-test-message` (`telegram-groups-routes.ts:313`) is additionally hard-blocked when `NODE_ENV === 'production'`.
- Hokim topic routes derive district scope exclusively from the session actor via `isDistrictScopedActor` (`hokim-topics-routes.ts:40-65`), never from the request.
- District-scoped AI operations enforce tenant matching via `allowMatchingDistrictOnly` (`require-auth.ts:143-154`).
- `schema/accounts.ts:24,29-31` CHECK constraints correctly forbid a PRODUCT_OWNER with a district and a DISTRICT_HOKIM without one.
- A suspended/disabled **account** is rejected at `session-manager.ts:99-102`, which also revokes its sessions.
- **No role escalation:** `packages/api-contracts/src/hokim-accounts.ts:13` pins the role as `z.literal('DISTRICT_HOKIM')` and `hokim-accounts-service.ts` hardcodes it at `:66,190,486`. No route accepts a client-supplied role, district assignment, or permission. No actor can modify its own or another's role.

#### Phase 1.3 — Tenant-Scoping Audit (districtId derivation)

Phase verdict: **no Critical, no High — ZERO confirmed cross-tenant leaks.** This is the direct verification of ADR 0006's accepted risk (no RLS), and the application-enforced scoping holds. 6 Medium findings, **all structural** (patterns that would leak under a plausible future refactor; none exploitable as written).

**Hokim-reachable surface is exactly two route groups** (hokim-topics, hokim-board). Every other route bearing a `:districtId` param is Product-Owner-only or an HMAC-signed webhook: `districts-routes.ts:32`, `hokim-accounts-routes.ts:28`, `subscriptions-routes.ts:73`, `telegram-bot-routes.ts:24`, `telegram-groups-routes.ts:143`, `userbot-session-routes.ts:112`, `health-routes.ts:246`, `issue-routes.ts:50`, `audit-routes.ts:18`, `district-topics-routes.ts:32`, `admin-signals-routes.ts:70`, `district-analysis-settings-routes.ts:51` → all `createRequireProductOwner`.

##### Scoping verified SAFE (highlights)

- `queryTopics` main list, both `EXISTS accepted_evidence` badge subqueries, and the `includeCount` count query all apply `district_id` on both sides of the join (`topic-query-engine.ts:242-259,277-308,312-323`).
- `queryHokimBoard` — district name, previous-visit read, and visit append all key on the actor's district (`topic-query-engine.ts:476-478,507-513,528-534`).
- `queryHokimStatistics` main CTE scopes `filtered_topics`, `evidence_counts`, and both `district_mahallas_total` legs (`topic-query-engine.ts:757-817`).
- All three `resolvePriorPeriodComparison` scopes (today / yesterday / custom range) apply the predicate (`topic-query-engine.ts:1073-1088,1121-1132,1189-1200`).
- `getTopicEvidence` topic-ownership gate, evidence count, evidence rows, group-name join, and settings read are all scoped (`topic-evidence-service.ts:231-238,243-250,251-270,263-264,271`).
- `districtAnalysisSettingsRepository` — every method filters district (`:63,82,100,114,130,172,187`).
- `aiOperationQueryService.listDistrictOperations` / `getDistrictOperationDetails` — scoped, with an explicit re-check at `:137`.
- `aiOperationRepository.findOperationsByDistrict` rejects an empty districtId (`:149`).
- `topic-retention-repository` — every query scoped plus a `validateDistrictScope` helper (`:43,85,114,125,133`).
- `healthService.getDistrictHealth` scoped (`:176-227`).
- Auth guards: `createRequireAuth` district/status checks (`require-auth.ts:81-139`) and `createRequireDistrictAccess` param match (`:143-154`) both correct.

##### Medium (all structural — no live exploit)

**M-7. Unscoped `topic_projections` read inside `getTopicEvidence`**
- Evidence: `modules/topics/topic-evidence-service.ts:239-241` — `db.query.topicProjections.findFirst({ where: eq(topicProjections.topicId, topicId) })`. `topic_projections` carries its own `district_id` (`schema/topic-projections.ts:15`) which is **not referenced**. The row feeds the response body at `:292-300` (`summary`, `lanes`) and `:354-355` (`anchorQuote`, `anchorEvidenceId`).
- Failure scenario: A Hokim of district A requests `GET /api/v1/hokim/topics/<topicId-of-B>/evidence`. District B's projection row **is fetched into server memory** by the unscoped query. No citizen text reaches the client **today** only because the district-scoped `topicRow` lookup (`:231-238`) returns `undefined` and `:274-276` throws `TopicNotFoundError` — a second, independent check is masking the missing predicate. Remove or weaken that gate and district B's synthesized topic summary leaks.
- Proposed fix: add `eq(topicProjections.districtId, actorContext.districtId)` to the `findFirst` where-clause — one line, mirroring the pattern already used for `acceptedEvidence` at `:248`.
- Confidence: **Confirmed** (predicate absent) / **not exploitable as written** — defence-in-depth failure

**M-8. `district-topics-routes.ts` synthesizes a `DistrictScopedActor` from an unverified URL param**
- Evidence: `modules/topics/district-topics-routes.ts:199-204` — `getTopicEvidence(db, { ...productOwner, districtId }, topicId, req.query)`, where `districtId` came from `req.params` (`:148`).
- Failure scenario: Today this route sits inside a `createRequireProductOwner` scope (`:32`), so only a deliberately cross-district Product Owner reaches it. But the service's type signature **promises** a `DistrictScopedActor`, while the caller fabricates one from a param the service never verifies. If this route is ever moved under `createRequireDistrictAccess`, the constructed object would satisfy the type with **no tenant assertion inside the service**.
- Proposed fix: keep the route PO-only and add an explicit `req.actor.role === 'PRODUCT_OWNER'` assertion in the handler, so the "PO is deliberately cross-district" intent is enforced rather than inferred from the surrounding scope.
- Confidence: Confirmed / structural

**M-9. `issueService.getOperationalIssueDetail` and its audit timeline have no district predicate**
- Evidence: `modules/issues/issue-service.ts:128-130` (`.where(eq(operationalIssues.id, issueId))`) and `:142-143` (audit timeline correlated only on `metadata->>'issueId'`). `operational_issues.district_id` exists (`schema/operational-issues.ts:20`) and is unused in both.
- Failure scenario: None currently — both reached only from `/api/v1/issues/:issueId` inside a `createRequireProductOwner` scope (`issue-routes.ts:50`). A future district-scoped caller of the same function would read another district's operational issue **plus its full audit timeline**.
- Proposed fix: accept a district scope and add the predicate (plus a correlated district filter on the timeline query); or rename to `getOperationalIssueDetailGlobal` so the absence of scoping is explicit at every call site.
- Confidence: Confirmed / structural

**M-10. Audit read services take `districtId` from the request query, not from the actor**
- Evidence: `modules/audit/audit-query-service.ts:480-484` and `:592-597` — `if (params.districtId) { conditions.push(eq(auditEvents.districtId, params.districtId)) }`; the value arrives from `req.query` via `audit-routes.ts:48-51`.
- Failure scenario: Not exploitable now — `audit-routes.ts:18` guards the whole scope with `createRequireProductOwner`. Note the interaction is **fail-closed**: `createRequireDistrictAccess` matches `req.params.districtId` (`require-auth.ts:144`) and this route has no `:districtId` path segment, so a Hokim would get 403, not another district's trail. The gap is that **no service-level check backstops a future guard change**.
- Proposed fix: pass `req.actor` into `queryAuditEvents` and derive `districtId` inside the service when the actor is district-scoped, rejecting a conflicting query param.
- Confidence: Confirmed / not exploitable as written

**M-11. Two coexisting scoping conventions with no compiler boundary between them**
- Evidence: Hokim topic services take a **narrowed actor** and derive scope internally (`topic-query-engine.ts:470-478,600-614,716-721`; `topic-evidence-service.ts:190-195,231-238`), while these take a **bare `districtId` string**: `queryDistrictMahallas` (`topic-query-engine.ts:165`), `queryDistrictTopicsPage` (`:414-420`), `aiOperationQueryService.listDistrictOperations` (`:100-104`), `getDistrictOperationDetails` (`:122-126`), `findOperationsByDistrict` (`ai-operation-repository.ts:145`), `aggregateHealthMetrics` (`:255-257`), `listSignals` (`topic-evidence-management-service.ts:112,178`), `getDistrictHealth` (`health-service.ts:176`).
- Failure scenario: Any of these functions imported by a new route whose guard forgets the district check will happily return another district's data. ADR 0006 removed the database backstop, so the **service signature is the only remaining place a mistake could be caught** — and half the codebase does not provide it.
- Proposed fix: standardize on `DistrictScopedActor` (or a branded `DistrictScope` type) for every district-scoped read; for the PO console require an explicit `CrossDistrictScope` marker so the two are not interchangeable at compile time.
- Confidence: Confirmed / structural

**M-12. Unfiltered scan paths permit a full cross-district sweep when called without a district scope**
- Evidence: `topic-reconciliation-service.ts:59-60` scopes only `if (options.districtId)` and is unfiltered otherwise; `topicEvidenceManagementService.listSignals` scopes only `if (query.districtId)` (`topic-evidence-management-service.ts:178-180`); `aiOperationRepository.findOperations` sets `baseWhere = undefined` when the filter is empty (`:78-117`).
- Failure scenario: Each is currently reached only from a Product-Owner-guarded route or a system job, so all districts at once is intended. The risk is that an unfiltered default is the **unsafe default**: a new district-scoped caller that omits the optional argument silently gets every district's rows instead of an error.
- Proposed fix: make the district scope a required argument and force the cross-district case to be an explicit `ALL_DISTRICTS` sentinel, so omission fails to compile rather than failing open.
- Confidence: Confirmed / structural

*(Phase 1.3 report was truncated at 23,264 chars; its "Verified sound" tail, Cross-layer pointers and Open questions are unrecoverable — the subagent was run synchronously and is not continuable. The six findings above are complete.)*

#### Phase 1.4 — Secret Handling & PII

Phase verdict: **1 Critical.** The encryption primitives are correct (AES-256-GCM, fresh random IV per operation, auth tag verified before plaintext is returned), but **key management is fail-open** and two live credentials sit in plaintext — so the at-rest protection is defeatable rather than merely weak. 1 Critical, 5 High, 5 Medium, 4 Low.

> **C-1, F-2, F-4 and F-6 were re-verified by the orchestrator directly against source** rather than accepted on report: `token-cipher.ts:11-34,102-106` and `webhook-security.ts:12-13` confirm the fallback key, the absent key-version lookup, and the HMAC/AES key reuse; a repo-wide grep confirms `ENCRYPTION_KEY` appears **nowhere** as a boot-time assertion; `district-telegram-userbot-sessions.ts:12-18` confirms plaintext `phone_number`/`api_id`/`api_hash` beside an encrypted session string.

##### Critical

**C-1. `ENCRYPTION_KEY` silently falls back to a hardcoded public dev key whenever `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/adapters/crypto/token-cipher.ts:17` — `export const FALLBACK_DEV_KEY = 'dev_encryption_key_32_bytes_len!'`; `:27-34` — `const rawKey = (overrideKey ?? process.env.ENCRYPTION_KEY)?.trim(); if (!rawKey) { if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') { throw ... } return Buffer.from(FALLBACK_DEV_KEY, 'utf8'); }`. **No boot-time assertion exists**: grep for `ENCRYPTION_KEY`/`getEncryptionKey` across `apps/backend/src` returns only `token-cipher.ts` (definitions + 2 call sites), one comment in `adapters/crypto/index.ts:7`, and `webhook-security.ts:2,12`. Nothing in `entrypoints/http.ts`, `worker.ts` or `userbot.ts` resolves the key at startup. `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` sets `NODE_ENV: development`; `docker-compose.prod.yml` sets `production`.
- Failure scenario: The key is resolved **lazily, on the first encrypt/decrypt call** — so there is no startup failure to signal a missing secret. Any process where `NODE_ENV` is unset, misspelled, or cased differently (`Production`), or any new service added without the env line, encrypts every district Telegram bot token and every userbot session string under a constant that is published in this repository. An attacker holding a database dump (backup file, replica, or via any other injection) decrypts all bot tokens and all userbot session strings offline and takes over the citizen-ingestion accounts. The same key also derives the webhook secret (F-4), so the attacker can additionally forge intake posts. This is the only route by which the dev key reaches production, and it is a silent one.
- Proposed fix: Delete the fallback constant. Make `getEncryptionKey()` throw when `ENCRYPTION_KEY` is absent **unconditionally**, and call it once during process start in `http.ts`, `worker.ts` and `userbot.ts` so the process refuses to boot rather than failing on first message.
- Confidence: Confirmed (fallback path + absent boot assertion re-verified by the orchestrator). Exploitability depends on the production `.env` actually defining `ENCRYPTION_KEY` — see OQ-6.

##### High

**F-2. Telegram `api_hash` stored in plaintext and accepted unvalidated from the request body**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/adapters/db/schema/district-telegram-userbot-sessions.ts:14` — `apiHash: text('api_hash'),` with no ciphertext/IV/tag columns, unlike `sessionEncrypted`/`sessionIv`/`sessionTag` at `:15-17` on the same table; `:12-13` leave `phoneNumber` and `apiId` plaintext too. `modules/userbot-session/userbot-session-service.ts:193` — `apiHash: params.apiHash?.trim() || null`; `userbot-session-routes.ts:142`; `packages/api-contracts/src/userbot-session.ts:30` — `apiHash: z.string().optional()` with no length/charset/format constraint and no `.min(1)`; `userbot-bootstrap-service.ts:85`.
- Failure scenario: The session string is encrypted but the API hash authorising the same application is not. A read-only leak of this table yields `api_hash` + plaintext phone number, with only the session string left encrypted — the at-rest protection is therefore already half-defeated for that record. The value is also visible to any operator running SQL, and because it is unvalidated a typo silently produces a session that fails only at bootstrap time.
- Proposed fix: Encrypt `api_hash` with the existing token cipher and tighten the contract to `z.string().regex(/^[0-9a-f]{32}$/)`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**F-3. Key version is persisted but never consulted on decrypt, so key rotation is impossible**
- Evidence: `token-cipher.ts:11-15` — `DecryptTokenPayload` has `encryptedToken`/`tokenIv`/`tokenTag` only, **no key-version field**; `:102-106` — `decryptToken(payload, customKey?)` calls `getEncryptionKey(customKey)` with no version lookup. Version is written but never read: `schema/district-telegram-bots.ts:18` and `district-telegram-userbot-sessions.ts:18` (`.notNull().default('v1')`); `userbot-session-service.ts:180,461` and `telegram-bot-service.ts:174,209` write `encrypted.tokenKeyVersion`, always the literal `'v1'` from `token-cipher.ts:76`.
- Failure scenario: Every ciphertext is stamped `v1` and decryption always uses whatever `ENCRYPTION_KEY` is currently set. Rotating the key invalidates every stored token and session at once with no way to read the old ciphertexts — so in practice the operator will never rotate, and a key exposed through C-1 or a leaked `.env` stays usable forever. The version column creates a false impression that rotation is supported. This is what turns C-1 from "fix the config" into "re-issue every credential".
- Proposed fix: Make `decryptToken` accept the stored key version and resolve it through a versioned key map (`ENCRYPTION_KEY_V1`, `ENCRYPTION_KEY_V2`, …); thread `tokenKeyVersion`/`sessionKeyVersion` from the row into every decrypt call site (`userbot-session-service.ts:270`, `telegram-groups-service.ts:246,441`, `telegram-watchdog.ts:83`, `cli/register-webhook.ts:24`).
- Confidence: Confirmed (re-verified by the orchestrator)

**F-4. Webhook authentication secret is derived from the same master key used for AES-GCM encryption**
- Evidence: `modules/telegram-intake/webhook-security.ts:12-13` — `const key = getEncryptionKey(); return crypto.createHmac('sha256', key).update(botId).digest('hex');` (re-verified). `telegram-intake-routes.ts:39-41` verifies against `deriveWebhookSecret(botId)`. Same key used for AES-256-GCM at `token-cipher.ts:79-81`.
- Failure scenario: One value serves both as an AES key and as an HMAC key, so any exposure of the encryption key also forges intake authentication. Combined with C-1, the dev constant yields a predictable `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` for any bot id, letting an unauthenticated external party POST fabricated citizen reports into `/api/v1/webhooks/telegram/:botId` and poison the civic-signal pipeline. Key reuse across two primitives is a hygiene defect independent of C-1.
- Proposed fix: Introduce a separate `WEBHOOK_SECRET_KEY` environment variable and derive with it; never reuse `ENCRYPTION_KEY` for HMAC.
- Confidence: Confirmed (re-verified by the orchestrator)

**F-5. Plaintext temporary passwords returned in HTTP response bodies**
- Evidence: `modules/hokim-accounts/hokim-accounts-routes.ts:72,91,145` — `reply.status(201|200).send(result)` with the full service return value; `hokim-accounts-service.ts:224,323,521` include `temporaryPassword`; `packages/api-contracts/src/hokim-accounts.ts:44,50,61` make `temporaryPassword: z.string().min(15).max(128)` a **required response field**, so this is by design, not an accident. Not persisted or audited: `audit-service.ts` `SENSITIVE_KEYS` includes `temporarypassword`, and metadata at `hokim-accounts-service.ts:211-216,309-315,507-513` carries only ids/usernames.
- Failure scenario: The Product Owner's browser receives the Hokim's new password in a JSON body, so it lands in browser devtools network logs, any client-side error reporter, any proxy access log configured with body capture, and on screen before the intended recipient uses it. It is single-use and forced-change-on-first-login, which bounds the damage, but it is a live credential with no channel binding to its recipient — and the same response carries the username, so a leaked response is directly usable. *(This closes the Phase 1.1/1.3 pointer: the value is a deliberate API contract, not an oversight — the finding is the delivery channel, not the presence.)*
- Proposed fix: Keep the response shape for contract compatibility but deliver the password out-of-band, or require a short-lived one-time retrieval (store an argon2 hash server-side with a TTL, let the Hokim fetch it once, then burn it). At minimum set `Cache-Control: no-store` on these three routes.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**F-6. Webhook secret token printed to stdout by the operator CLI**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/cli/register-webhook.ts:31` — `const secretToken = deriveWebhookSecret(row.bot_id);`; `:35` sets `webhookPayload.secret_token = secretToken`; `:42` — `console.log('Payload configuration:', JSON.stringify(webhookPayload, null, 2));`.
- Failure scenario: Running `pnpm cli:register-webhook` writes the live webhook authentication secret into the terminal, into any `tee`d output file, and into shell scrollback. Anyone with that value can post fabricated intake updates for that bot until `ENCRYPTION_KEY` changes — and per F-3 the key cannot practically be changed. Compounds F-4 and C-1.
- Proposed fix: Print `secret_token: '[set]'` instead of the value; log only the URL and `drop_pending_updates`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

##### Medium

**F-7. Logger flattens Error objects by spreading all own enumerable properties into the log line** — `utils/logger.ts:12-22` — `return { message: err.message, name: err.name, stack: err.stack, ...(err as unknown as Record<string, unknown>) };`. Raw error objects are passed at `adapters/telegram/userbot-client-adapter.ts:140,225` and `modules/userbot/userbot-connection-manager.ts:275,329`. Any error carrying extra own properties — a GramJS/MTProto error with request or connection metadata, a wrapped driver error with `payload`/`config`/`response` — is serialised verbatim to stdout. Fix: whitelist `message`/`name`/`stack` instead of spreading. Confidence: Confirmed (mechanism); the specific live emission is an unverified hypothesis.

**F-8. Error envelope forwards `details` and `blockers` to the client even on 5xx** — `modules/errors/api-error-envelope.ts:176-179` reads `details` and `:171-174` reads `blockers` with **no `statusCode < 500` guard**, unlike the deliberately hardened message path at `:130-136` (`resolveErrorMessage`). The contract permits arbitrary content: `packages/api-contracts/src/common.ts:42` — `details: z.record(z.unknown()).optional()`. Latent today (observed 5xx producers attach no `details`), but any future 500 attaching diagnostic context is returned verbatim; PostgreSQL's `detail` routinely contains the conflicting key value. Fix: gate both blocks on `statusCode < 500`. Confidence: Confirmed.

**F-9. Failed sign-in audit metadata records the attempted username** — `modules/auth/auth-service.ts:122-127` (`{ username, reason: 'ACCOUNT_NOT_FOUND' }`) and `:135-143,151-159,176-189` (same for `INVALID_PASSWORD`, `ACCOUNT_DISABLED`, `DISTRICT_NOT_ACTIVE`). `SENSITIVE_KEYS` in `modules/audit/audit-service.ts:9-34` does not include `username`, so nothing redacts it. Exposed via `audit-routes.ts:24-53` (`GET /api/v1/audit/events`). Failure scenario: the audit trail distinguishes "username exists, wrong password" from "username does not exist" by `reason`, letting anyone with audit read access enumerate valid usernames and map districts to Hokim usernames — reconnaissance for a targeted credential attack on the live system. Fix: record `reason` plus a boolean `accountFound`; drop `username` from failure metadata (or store it hashed). Confidence: Confirmed.

**F-10. Userbot phone number written to audit metadata and CLI stdout, and stored plaintext** — `modules/userbot-session/userbot-session-service.ts:212-216` puts `phoneNumber: params.phoneNumber.trim()` into audit metadata; `SENSITIVE_KEYS` (`audit-service.ts:9-34`) has no phone-number entry so `sanitizeMetadata` passes it through; `cli/bootstrap-userbot-session.ts:120` prints it; `schema/district-telegram-userbot-sessions.ts:12` stores it plaintext. Failure scenario: the userbot account belongs to a real person in the district — their personal number sits unencrypted in the primary table, is copied into the audit table (queryable through the PO audit UI), and is printed to the terminal during bootstrap. ADR 0009 accepts shared-credential blast radius but says nothing about the holder's personal number in two places. Fix: drop `phoneNumber` from audit metadata (`sessionId` already identifies the record); encrypt the column or mask to last four digits. Confidence: Confirmed.

**F-11. Intake telemetry bypasses the structured logger and emits Telegram identifiers to stdout** — `modules/telegram-intake/telegram-intake-routes.ts:134-146` — `console.log('[telemetry:telegram-intake]', { event, botId, districtId, mahallaName, chatId: result.chatId, messageId: result.messageId, ... })`, repeated at `:83-91,100-108,156-168,178-188,197-206`. `utils/logger.ts:36` suppresses the structured logger under `NODE_ENV=test`, but `console.log` is not routed through it. Failure scenario: chat id, message id, mahalla name and district id are written to container stdout on every accepted, duplicate and dropped update, outside any log-level control and outside the test-suppression guard; under Docker's default json-file driver these accumulate indefinitely, and `mahallaName` + `chatId` identify a neighbourhood group and locate a specific citizen message. Fix: route through `logger.info` and demote `chatId`/`messageId` to a production-off debug level, or hash them. Confidence: Confirmed.

##### Low

**F-12. Development compose ships a working default database password** — `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:8` — `POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:-mahalla_dev_password}`, with the same default embedded in `DATABASE_URL` at `:34`; port published at `"${POSTGRES_PORT:-5433}:5432"`. A dev stack started on a network-reachable host exposes PostgreSQL with a repository-visible password. Fix: use `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?Database password required}`, matching `docker-compose.prod.yml:8`. Confidence: Confirmed

**F-13. CORS origin falls back to localhost when `APP_ORIGIN` is unset** — `entrypoints/http.ts:134` — `process.env.APP_ORIGIN || 'http://localhost:5173'`; `modules/auth/origin-guard.ts:73`. A fail-open default on a security control: a misconfigured production deploy degrades silently instead of being loudly rejected. Fix: throw at startup when `APP_ORIGIN` is unset in production; keep the localhost default only when `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`. Confidence: Confirmed

**F-14. Hardcoded ngrok hostname and bot id in a CLI entrypoint** — `cli/register-webhook.ts:9` — `process.argv[2] || 'https://mulled-revivable-satirical.ngrok-free.dev'`; `:16` — hardcoded bot id `['8293431272']` in the SELECT. Running the CLI with no argument silently repoints a production bot's webhook at a third-party tunnel — and this is the same script that prints the webhook secret (F-6). Fix: make both required arguments; delete the literals. Confidence: Confirmed

**F-15. Driver error code echoed verbatim in the client error envelope** — `modules/errors/api-error-envelope.ts:122-128` — `resolveErrorCode` returns any non-empty string `error.code`, while `resolveStatusCode` (`:114-120`) has already defaulted to 500, so a PostgreSQL violation reaches the client as `error.code: "23505"` instead of `INTERNAL_ERROR`. Minor internal-detail disclosure (confirms Postgres and that a unique constraint fired). Fix: honour `error.code` only for known domain codes; force `INTERNAL_ERROR` on 5xx. Confidence: Confirmed

##### Verified sound

- AES-256-GCM with a fresh random 12-byte IV per operation, auth tag extracted after `final()`: `adapters/crypto/token-cipher.ts:80-87`; tag verified via `setAuthTag` before plaintext is returned: `:111-117`.
- Key material length-validated to exactly 32 bytes with a loud error otherwise: `token-cipher.ts:45-49`.
- Bot token display value masked to the public numeric bot id only: `token-cipher.ts:59-68`; `district-telegram-bots.ts:19`. Bot token never appears in Telegram API error text: `adapters/telegram/telegram-client.ts:36-42,112`.
- Public userbot session projection strictly omits ciphertext, IV, tag and key version: `userbot-session-service.ts:113-127,233-248`.
- 5xx responses never carry the thrown message or a stack: `api-error-envelope.ts:130-136`; global handler sends only the serialised envelope: `entrypoints/http.ts:151-168`.
- Session tokens stored as SHA-256 hashes only; raw token exists solely in the cookie: `modules/auth/session-manager.ts:11-18,42-51`.
- Fastify request logging excludes bodies and headers; global error handler logs only `err`, `reqId`, `method`, `url`: `entrypoints/http.ts:93-101,151-161`. No route logs `req.body`.
- `.gitignore` covers `.env`/`.env.*` with a `!.env.example` exception; `git ls-files` matching `env` returns only `deploy/compose/.env.example`, which contains placeholders only. **No real `.env` is tracked and no secret literal is committed.**
- Audit metadata scrubber drops keys normalised to password/token/secret/jwt/cookie/authorization/apikey/bottoken/temporarypassword, recursively, and redacts token-shaped strings: `modules/audit/audit-service.ts:9-42,44-70,156`.
- AI payloads guarded against forbidden privacy keys (`residentText`, `verbatimText`, `citizenText`, `telegramHandle`, `botToken`, `apiKey`, `rawHeaders`, `authSecret`) via `assertPrivacyBoundary`: `modules/ai/ai-operation-query-service.ts:44-94`.
- Webhook secret comparison hashes both sides to fixed 32-byte length then `crypto.timingSafeEqual`: `webhook-security.ts:22-38`. Driver error telemetry scrubbed of `VALUES(...)`, `bot<id>:<token>` and `password='...'`: `webhook-security.ts:44-64`.
- Database connection strings masked before printing: `adapters/db/client.ts:32-34`; DB target fail-closed with no implicit DSN fallback: `client.ts:15-24`.
- Temporary passwords from a CSPRNG, Fisher-Yates shuffled with CSPRNG values, re-validated against policy before return: `adapters/crypto/temporary-password.ts:14-31,43-71`.
- Crypto facade deliberately does not re-export `getEncryptionKey`/`FALLBACK_DEV_KEY` into domain code: `adapters/crypto/index.ts:6-9`.

---

### Layer 2 — Data Integrity & Persistence

#### Phase 2.1 — Schema & Migration Safety

Phase verdict: The schema is **unusually disciplined for a live system** — 22 tables, 29 journal-registered migrations, 100% `withTimezone: true` timestamps (60+ columns), CHECK constraints on most status columns, and `district_id` indexed on *every* district-scoped table. Zero Critical. Three real defects, all in what the schema *does not* protect. 3 High, 8 Medium, 5 Low.

##### High

**H-4. Deleting a district cascades away that district's entire audit history**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/adapters/db/schema/audit.ts:8-10` — `districtId` references `districts.id` with `onDelete: 'cascade'`; migration `apps/backend/drizzle/0012_heavy_human_robot.sql:2` — `... ON DELETE cascade ON UPDATE no action` (both **re-verified by the orchestrator**). `modules/subscriptions/district-deletion-service.ts:306` deletes the `districts` row outright.
- Failure scenario: Deleting a district (or any operator `DELETE FROM districts`) silently removes every `audit_events` row scoped to it — disclosure confirmations, bot connect/disconnect, Hokim account lifecycle, settings activations. The deletion *proof* survives (`district_deletion_records` deliberately has no FK to `districts`, `district-deletion-records.ts:8`) and the global `DISTRICT_LIVE_DELETED` event survives (`district-deletion-service.ts:309-324` writes `districtId: null`), but the **per-district forensic trail is unrecoverable**. This is not an accepted trade-off: no ADR mentions audit preservation on deletion (the only ADR hits for deletion are ADR 0007 on session purge and ADR 0009's pointer to AD-11 deletion reconciliation).
- Proposed fix: Change to `onDelete: 'set null'` in `audit.ts` and migrate: drop the FK, re-add with `ON DELETE set null`. The existing `audit_events_district_created_at_id_idx` (`0014_closed_spirit.sql:41`) keeps `districtId = NULL` rows queryable by the global cursor path.
- Confidence: Confirmed (orchestrator-verified). *Overlaps Phase 2.4 (deletion semantics) — count once, here.*

**H-5. Plaintext Telegram API credentials beside an encrypted session blob**
- Evidence: `adapters/db/schema/district-telegram-userbot-sessions.ts:12-18` — `phone_number` NOT NULL, `api_id` NOT NULL, `api_hash` nullable, all plain `text`, while `session_encrypted`/`session_iv`/`session_tag`/`session_key_version` on the same row are the ciphertext/tag fields; migration `0026_sweet_turbo.sql:5-10` matches. `packages/api-contracts/src/userbot-session.ts:15-16` deliberately exposes `phoneNumber`/`apiId` to the API surface.
- Failure scenario: A database dump, replica or backup leak hands an attacker `api_id` + `api_hash` + `phone_number` in directly usable plaintext — the exact triple needed to re-authenticate the userbot account outside the app. The *envelope* fields are protected while the *identity* fields are not, which reads as partial remediation. **Same defect as Phase 1.4 F-2, independently re-derived from the migration side**; H-5 adds that `api_id` and `phone_number` are also unprotected, not just `api_hash`.
- Proposed fix: Add `api_id_*`/`api_hash_*`/`phone_number_*` ciphertext/IV/tag columns reusing `token_key_version`, backfill, then drop the plaintext columns. Keep the public contract returning `apiId`/`phoneNumber` by decrypting at the repository boundary. Coordinate with C-1: the same fail-open key resolver would protect these fields.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**H-6. Core pipeline enum columns have no DB CHECK at all**
- Evidence: `adapters/db/schema/topics.ts:14` (`primary_lane`), `topic-projections.ts:23` (`primary_lane`), `ai.ts:22,23,45,52,89` (`ai_profiles.operation_type`/`provider`, `ai_operations.operation_type`/`final_status`, `ai_provider_attempts.status`), `accepted-evidence.ts:27` (`content_type`) — none appear in any `drizzle/*.sql` (grep for `CHECK` across migrations). The contracts are strict and exist: `packages/api-contracts/src/topics.ts:4-10` (`QualifyingLaneSchema`), `analysis-settings.ts:4-9`, `signals.ts:20`.
- Failure scenario: A migration, backfill or manual `UPDATE` writes `'WATER_SUPPLY'` into `topics.primary_lane`. Reads cast blindly — `topic-query-engine.ts:373` does `(row.primaryLane as QualifyingLane) || 'HOKIM_RELATED'`, an unchecked `as`, so the bad value flows into `TopicCardItemSchema` and fails zod **at serialization**, producing a 500 on a district board instead of a caught data error. `ai_operations.final_status` drives STALE/COMPLETED branching and is typed only in a comment (`ai.ts:52`).
- Proposed fix: One migration adding CHECKs mirroring the contract enums exactly, using the `DO $$ ... EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object` guard already established at `0023_intake_status.sql:2-6`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

##### Medium

**M-13. Migration 0027 fails on re-run — the only unguarded recent migration** — `apps/backend/drizzle/0027_broken_molecule_man.sql:1-2`: `ADD COLUMN "transport"` with no `IF NOT EXISTS`, and a CHECK added outside any `DO $$ ... EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object` block. Every neighbouring migration uses the guard (`0023:2-6`, `0024:1-6`, `0025:3-7`, `0026:18-22`, `0028:1-5`). Any replay (disaster-restore reconciliation, shadow rebuild, manual re-apply after partial failure) aborts with `column "transport" ... already exists`, rolling back and wedging the deploy. Fix: `IF NOT EXISTS` + guarded CHECK. Confidence: Confirmed

**M-14. Nine of 29 migrations have no Drizzle snapshot — `drizzle-kit generate` will drift** — `apps/backend/drizzle/meta/` holds snapshots for 0000-0004, 0007-0015, 0018, 0019, 0025-0028; **missing 0005, 0006, 0016, 0017, 0020-0024** (9, **verified by orchestrator via glob**), while `_journal.json` lists all 29. The missing set is exactly the hand-authored migrations. The next `drizzle-kit generate` diffs against the 0028 snapshot, which never saw `0005`/`0006`'s columns (`accounts.status`, `accounts.district_id`, `accounts.must_change_password`, `districts.activated_at`, `districts.activated_by_id`), so it will emit SQL re-adding existing columns and **fail mid-deploy**. Invisible until that command runs. Fix: regenerate the nine snapshots before the next schema change. Confidence: Confirmed

**M-15. `accounts.district_id` cascade removes accounts and sessions on district deletion** — `accounts.ts:14-16` (`onDelete: 'cascade'`), migration `0005_warm_hokim_accounts.sql:3`; `sessions.accountId` cascades too (`sessions.ts:8-10`). Intended for live deletion, but it also fires on an accidental `DELETE FROM districts` with no `restrict` guard, and the partial-unique `accounts_active_district_hokim_idx` (`accounts.ts:33-35`) means the cascade succeeds silently. Note `audit_events.actor_id` is a bare `text` with no FK (`audit.ts:11`), so actor identity survives as a dangling reference. Fix: none required for the intended flow; switch to `set null` only if Hokim accounts are meant to outlive their district (which `accounts_role_district_check` currently forbids). Confidence: Confirmed

**M-16. `topics`/`topic_projections` cursor ordering has no supporting index** — `topic-query-engine.ts:392-395` builds a keyset cursor on `(latestMeaningfulActivityTimestamp, id)`, but `topic_projections` indexes are `topic_id` (unique), `(district_id, calendar_day)`, `(district_id, mahalla_name, calendar_day)` — none includes the cursor column. The `topics` index on `(districtId, latestRelevantEvidenceTimestamp)` is on a *different column of a different table*. Every board page sorts the full district projection set rather than walking an index. Fix: `CREATE INDEX ... ON topic_projections (district_id, latest_meaningful_activity_timestamp DESC, id DESC)`. Confidence: Confirmed (index absence verified; query-plan impact inferred)

**M-17. Zod max-length contracts with no DB length enforcement** — `verbatimText` max 4000 (`signals.ts:94`) vs unbounded `text` (`accepted-evidence.ts:26`); `changeReason` max 500; district name max 100 (`districts.ts:34-36`) vs `districts.ts:9`; `externalPaymentReference` 255 / `internalNote` 2000 (`subscriptions.ts:51,56`) vs bare `text` (`district-subscriptions.ts:16-17`); mahalla name 100 / chat id 50 (`telegram-groups.ts:44,52`); `username` 64 (`hokim-accounts.ts:32`) vs `accounts.ts:10`. API-layer validation means the exposure is **internal writes** — seeds, backfills, batch importers, direct SQL. A 100 KB `verbatim_text` succeeds at the DB and breaks every downstream consumer assuming the 4000 cap (UI, AI prompt assembly, export). Fix: `CHECK (length(col) <= N)` on the columns where the contract is authoritative. Confidence: Confirmed

**M-18. Enum CHECK ↔ contract mismatches on `operational_issues`** — `packages/api-contracts/src/health.ts:26-38` defines 11 `ComponentTypeEnumSchema` values and `:7-14` six `HealthStatusEnumSchema` values, but `operational_issues.component` (`operational-issues.ts:23`) and `.health_status` (`:27`) have **no CHECK**; `issue_category` (`:24`) likewise has none against the 18-value `IssueCategoryEnumSchema` (`issues.ts:28-47`). Only `scope`/`severity`/`status` are checked (`:40-55`, migration `0013:21-24`). A new category value gets persisted and the issues list fails *wholesale* rather than degrading one row, because `OperationalIssueSchema` validates on read. Fix: three CHECKs mirroring the enums (accepting the lockstep maintenance cost). Confidence: Confirmed

**M-19. `district_subscriptions.status` can diverge from `districts.status`** — two independent copies of one lifecycle state: `districts.ts:11,27-30` (`DEFAULT 'SETUP_INCOMPLETE'`, 5-value CHECK) and `district-subscriptions.ts:12,24-27` (same five values, own CHECK), one row each per district, backfilled once at migration time (`0016:19-35`). Nothing at DB level keeps them in sync — a transition updating only `district_subscriptions` leaves `districts.status` stale, and since `access_eligible` (`districts.ts:12`) and readiness logic read `districts`, a district can be `SUSPENDED` in one table and `ACTIVE` in the other. Postgres CHECKs cannot express cross-table equality. Fix: make one table authoritative (drop the other's column) or add a mirroring trigger in the same transaction. Confidence: Confirmed

**M-20. `audit_events` metadata search is unindexed across 26 keys** — `modules/audit/audit-query-service.ts:556-563` issues `ilike` on `id`, `action`, and a loop over `ALLOWED_METADATA_SEARCH_KEYS` (`packages/api-contracts/src/audit.ts:117-143`, 26 keys) producing `metadata->key ILIKE pattern`. No GIN index on `audit_events.metadata` exists in any migration (grep for `gin` returns nothing). One search = up to 28 `ILIKE` predicates each requiring a jsonb extraction per row, over a district's whole audit slice, on a table with no retention path. Degrades from fast to multi-second as history grows. Fix: `CREATE INDEX ... USING gin (metadata jsonb_path_ops)`; consider `pg_trgm` for the `%…%` pattern, which the GIN jsonb index does not accelerate. Confidence: Confirmed

##### Low

**L-9. `sign_in_rate_limits` has no cleanup index** — `rate-limits.ts:14-17` indexes only `key` and `locked_until`; `rate-limiter.ts:109` deletes only on the exact-key reset path, so rows accumulate one per `(ip, username)` forever. The existing `locked_until` index is the wrong predicate for an "idle longer than N days" sweep (that needs `updated_at`). Item 9 of the brief is otherwise satisfied: `key` is `NOT NULL UNIQUE` (`:7`), giving `onConflictDoUpdate({ target: key })` a valid arbiter, and the upsert is genuinely atomic. Fix: index `updated_at` + a retention job. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-10. `user_dashboard_visits` is an unpruned append log — and the queued pointer is REFUTED** — `topic-query-engine.ts:528-534` inserts a fresh `vis_<uuid>` PK on every board GET; `user-visits.ts:20-24` indexes `(userId, districtId, visitedAt)`; nothing deletes old rows. The in-code comment at `:522-527` claiming no unique `(userId, districtId)` constraint is **accurate** (`user-visits.ts:18-25`, migration `0011:11`). Because the insert always supplies a new random PK and never targets a conflict, there are **no duplicate rows and no lost upsert** — the append is intentional and correct. *(This retires the Phase 1.1/1.3 pointer: it was logged as a suspected schema defect and is not one.)* Real consequence is unbounded growth, one row per board load per user, forever. Fix: retention delete older than the freshness window; no schema change. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-11. `district_deletion_records.districtId` is intentionally not a foreign key** — `district-deletion-records.ts:8` has no `.references()`; migration `0018:3` likewise. **Deliberate and correct**: the record must outlive the deleted district, which is exactly why H-4's audit cascade is the inconsistency. Cost: a typo'd `district_id` is accepted, and the unique index (`:25`) then permanently blocks a correct record for the real district. Fix: none; documented as an intentional asymmetry. Confidence: Confirmed

**L-12. `district_subscriptions.scheduled_transition_at` indexed without a status predicate** — `district-subscriptions.ts:33`, migration `0017:5`. The index covers settled `CANCELLED`/`SUSPENDED` rows, so the scheduler revisits them each tick. Fix: make it partial with `WHERE scheduled_transition_at IS NOT NULL AND status NOT IN ('CANCELLED')`. Confidence: Unverified hypothesis (scheduler query not read in this phase)

**L-13. `district_analysis_settings_versions` lacks a single-active guard** — `ai.ts:126-134` has a unique on `(districtId, version)` and a **non-unique** index on `(districtId, isActive)` with no partial predicate, unlike the correct pattern used twice elsewhere (`accounts_active_district_hokim_idx` at `accounts.ts:33-35`, `operational_issues_active_logical_key_uidx` at `operational-issues.ts:57-59`). A failed activation or retry can leave two `isActive = true` rows, and the activation service's "the active version" read becomes a nondeterministic pick — the district silently analyzes against the wrong vocabulary. Fix: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... (district_id) WHERE is_active`. Confidence: Confirmed (index shape); whether activation logic already prevents it transactionally belongs to Phase 2.2

##### Schema inventory

22 live tables (the `global_analysis_settings_*` tables created in `0014` were dropped in `0021`).

| Table | PK | FKs (onDelete) | Notable missing protection |
|---|---|---|---|
| `accounts` | id | →districts (cascade) | — |
| `sessions` | id | →accounts (cascade) | — |
| `audit_events` | id | →districts (**cascade ⚠ H-4**) | GIN on metadata (M-20); no retention |
| `sign_in_rate_limits` | id | none | cleanup index (L-9) |
| `districts` | id | →accounts ×2 (set null) | — |
| `district_telegram_bots` | id | →districts (cascade) | — |
| `district_telegram_groups` | id | →districts (cascade) | — |
| `district_telegram_userbot_sessions` | id | →districts (cascade) | **plaintext api_id/api_hash/phone (H-5)** |
| `telegram_intake_records` | id | →districts (cascade) | — |
| `ai_profiles` | id | none | CHECKs on operation_type/provider (H-6) |
| `ai_operations` | id | →districts (cascade), →ai_profiles (no action) | CHECKs on operation_type/final_status (H-6) |
| `ai_provider_attempts` | id | →ai_operations (cascade) | CHECK on status (H-6) |
| `district_analysis_settings_versions` | id | →districts (cascade), →accounts (set null) | partial-unique single active (L-13) |
| `district_analysis_settings_drafts` | id | →districts (cascade), →versions (set null), →accounts (set null) | — |
| `topics` | id | →districts (cascade) | CHECK on primary_lane (H-6) |
| `accepted_evidence` | id | →topics (**restrict**), →districts (cascade), →intake (no action), →ai_operations (set null) | CHECK on content_type (H-6) |
| `topic_projections` | id | →topics (cascade), →districts (cascade), →accepted_evidence (**restrict**), →ai_profiles (no action), →ai_operations (set null) | cursor-order index (M-16); CHECK on primary_lane (H-6) |
| `user_dashboard_visits` | id | →accounts (cascade), →districts (cascade) | retention purge (L-10) |
| `operational_issues` | id | →districts (cascade) | CHECKs on component/health_status/issue_category (M-18) |
| `district_subscriptions` | id | →districts (cascade) | partial index (L-12); no sync with districts.status (M-19) |
| `district_deletion_records` | id | **none** (intentional, L-11) | — |

##### Verified sound

- **All 29 migrations journal-registered** with contiguous idx 0–28 (`drizzle/meta/_journal.json`). On-disk lexical order puts `0027` before `0026`, but journal indices are correct and Drizzle applies by index.
- **Every timestamp column is `withTimezone: true`** across all 18 schema files — zero naive timestamps (60+ columns checked).
- **Tenant-scoping `district_id` is indexed on every district-scoped table** — verified individually; no unindexed `district_id` found. This is what keeps ADR 0006's hand-written scoping performant.
- **The two `restrict` FKs are correctly chosen**: `accepted_evidence.topic_id` and `topic_projections.anchor_evidence_id` block deletion, protecting legal civic evidence from cascading loss. Cleanup order `topic_projections → accepted_evidence → topics` matches (`district-deletion-service.ts:271`).
- **Two partial-unique indexes correctly encode single-active invariants**: `accounts_active_district_hokim_idx`, `operational_issues_active_logical_key_uidx`.
- **No enum CHECK contradicts its contract** — every existing CHECK matches its zod enum exactly (districts, accounts, bots, groups, userbot sessions, intake, issues, deletion records). The findings are *missing* CHECKs, not wrong ones.
- **`sign_in_rate_limits` fully supports its atomic upsert** (item 9 of the brief satisfied).
- **No `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` anywhere**, so every migration is transaction-safe; all are DDL-only plus one idempotent backfill (`0016:19-35` uses `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and correctly coalesces timestamps).
- **`defaultNow()` double-writes are deliberate** where they occur (`topic-query-engine.ts:532-533` sets in-memory and persisted values together). Not a defect.
- **`operational_issues` scope/district consistency is DB-enforced** (`operational-issues.ts:52-55`), the one cross-field invariant that is protected.

##### Cross-layer pointers

- **2.2**: `rate-limiter.ts:92-100` computes `lockedUntil` and applies it in a **second statement** after the upsert returns — a crash between them leaves `failedAttempts >= 5` with `lockedUntil` NULL, and `checkRateLimit` (`:35`) would report `isLocked: false`. Verify the request path re-reads.
- **2.2**: `district-deletion-service.ts:306` deletes `districts` inside the transaction after all cleaners — confirm the global audit append (`:309`) and tombstone read (`:326`) are in the same transaction, or the deletion proof can be lost.
- **2.3**: `telegram_intake_records.status = 'PROCESSING'` with `processed_at` NULL — is there a reaper for stuck rows? The burst index (`telegram-intakes.ts:70-75`) omits `status`, so a stuck-row scan cannot use it.
- **2.3**: `topics.required_derived_generation` vs `applied_derived_generation` (`topics.ts:22-23`) — the stuck-generation reconciliation path belongs to 2.3.
- **2.4**: `topics.retention_expires_at` (`topics.ts:19-21`) and its index (`:42`) exist, but no retention column was found on `accepted_evidence` or `topic_projections` — confirm the retention job traverses the `restrict` FKs correctly.
- **2.4**: `user_dashboard_visits` (L-10) and `sign_in_rate_limits` (L-9) have no retention owner.
- **2.4**: `district_deletion_records.protected_backup_expiry_deadline` / `backup_expiry_status` (`district-deletion-records.ts:16-18`) — whether the expiry job actually runs is 2.4's scope.
- **3.1**: `ai_operations.result_payload` jsonb (`ai.ts:53`) is documented as "Sanitized output metadata" — verify no raw model output or citizen text is persisted there.
- **3.2**: `accepted_evidence.user_metadata`/`reply_metadata` jsonb (`accepted-evidence.ts:28-29`) is documented as whitelisted (`telegramUserId, username, firstName, lastName`) but has **no DB-level key restriction**; enforcement is application-only.
- **4.3**: `operational_issues` retry fields (`isRetryEligible`, `retryCount`, `pendingRetry`, `lastRetryAt` in `issues.ts:93-96`) have **no corresponding columns** in `operational-issues.ts` — apparently derived at read time; confirm. **[RE-ROUTED to Layer 6 (6.1, contract/schema/web drift) — this is schema drift between a service contract and its table, not observability or audit-trail work, so it is neither Layer 4 (4.3) nor Layer 5 (5.1). The original label above is left unstruck for traceability.]**
- **6.1**: `sessionKeyVersion`/`tokenKeyVersion` default to `'v1'` (`district-telegram-userbot-sessions.ts:18`, `district-telegram-bots.ts:18`) — H-5's fix must extend key versioning to the identity fields.

##### Open questions (Phase 2.1)

- OQ-9 … OQ-13 raised by this phase were moved to the single top-level `## Open Questions` section. See OQ-9 … OQ-13 there.

---

#### Phase 2.2 — Transactional Correctness

Phase verdict: The core intake → AI → assignment → projection pipeline is transactionally sound and consistently uses the correct `withTransactionalIntake` same-connection pattern, but three defects break atomicity guarantees the codebase itself documents elsewhere — a permanently non-re-arming brute-force lockout, an irreversible silent message-loss window in burst debounce, and a stale-snapshot CAS check that runs outside its own transaction — plus a set of smaller multi-write sequences with no transaction at all.

**Counts: 1 Critical, 2 High, 4 Medium, 3 Low.**

> **Orchestrator reconciliation note.** The Phase 2.2 report arrived in two parts. Two defects in the delivered text need normalising before they are trusted as recorded here:
> 1. **M-22 and M-24 are the same finding** — PO account creation not being transactional (`account-service.ts:88-107`), reported twice under two IDs. Recorded once, as **M-22**; M-24 is used below for a genuinely distinct defect that the subagent had mislabelled `M-23`.
> 2. **`M-23` was used for two different subjects.** The first delivered part described sign-in's four-write sequence; the second described the post-commit backup-expiry enqueue. Recorded here as **M-23** (sign-in) and **M-24** (backup-expiry enqueue). No finding content was altered; only the IDs were disambiguated. Next free IDs are therefore **C-3, H-9, M-25, L-17**.
> 3. Every file path in the raw report had lost its separator (`src\modules` → `srcmodules`, `docs\adr` → `docsadr`). The subagent's own "correction" table reproduced the broken form in both columns. All paths below were **corrected by the orchestrator against `glob`/`grep` on disk** and are quoted in their true form.

##### Critical

**C-2. Burst-debounce marks intake records processed before enqueueing the downstream job, outside any transaction — permanent silent message loss with no repair path**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/jobs/burst-debounce-job-handler.ts:265` calls `burstBufferRepo.markBurstIntakesProcessed(db, allIntakeIds, batchId)` on the pool-backed `db` (autocommit, no transaction). The downstream job is only dispatched afterwards at `:306` via `sendQueueJob(boss, TELEGRAM_SEMANTIC_RELEVANCE_QUEUE, candidateData, ...)`. Neither write shares a transaction. The re-scan predicate is `burst-buffer-repository.ts:33` `isNull(telegramIntakeRecords.processedAt)`; the sole caller is `burst-debounce-job-handler.ts:83`. The excluded-record updates at `:247-257` are likewise separate autocommit writes.
- Failure scenario: A user's burst is flushed; `markBurstIntakesProcessed` commits at `:265`, setting `processed_at` on every message in the burst. Before `:306` executes the process crashes, or the pg-boss `send` fails hard. pg-boss retries the burst-debounce job. On retry, `getUnprocessedBurstIntakes` (`:83`) returns `[]` because `processed_at` is now non-NULL, so `:90-92` `continue`s and the job completes successfully. The messages are now permanently marked processed and will never be re-examined: the Telegram webhook was already ACKed (`telegram-intake-service.ts:314-325`), so no upstream redelivery occurs either. `topic-query-engine.ts:639-688` (`checkProcessingDelay`) only *reports* a delay via the Console; it performs no repair. The civic signal is silently and irrecoverably lost, with no audit record, no operational issue, and no alert.
- Proposed fix: Replace the two-step `db` writes + `boss.send` with one `withTransactionalIntake(pool, boss, async ({ tx, enqueueJob }) => ...)` block, passing `tx` to a tx-accepting variant of `getUnprocessedBurstIntakes`/`markBurstIntakesProcessed` and using `enqueueJob` for the semantic-relevance dispatch. The handler already receives `pool` in its deps shape (`:26-30`) only via `db`/`boss`; add `pool` to `BurstDebounceJobDeps` and thread it from `entrypoints/worker.ts:88-90`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

##### High

**H-7. Failed-attempt lockout never re-arms after its first expiry, yielding a recurring unlimited brute-force window**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/auth/rate-limiter.ts:66-72` resets `failed_attempts` to 1 only when `locked_until IS NOT NULL AND locked_until <= now - LOCKOUT_DURATION_MS`, i.e. only once the lock has been expired for a further 15 minutes. `:95` guards the re-arm with `if (isNowLocked && !result.lockedUntil)` — a *non-null check*, not an *expired check*. `checkRateLimit` locks only on `record.lockedUntil.getTime() > now` (`:35`), otherwise returns `isLocked: false` (`:40`).
- Failure scenario: Five failed logins set `failed_attempts = 5`, `locked_until = T+15m`. The attacker waits out the lock. At `T+15m+ε`, `checkRateLimit` reads the expired `locked_until` and returns `isLocked: false` (`:35`/`:40`), admitting the attempt. `recordFailedAttempt` now sees `locked_until` non-null but not yet 15 minutes past expiry, so the reset CASE (`:66-72`) does not fire and `failed_attempts` increments to 6 → `isNowLocked = true` (`:92`). But `result.lockedUntil` is non-null (stale), so the guard at `:95` is false and the `UPDATE ... set({lockedUntil})` at `:96-99` is **skipped** — the expired timestamp is never refreshed. The function returns `isLocked: true` for that single request only; every subsequent request re-reads the still-expired `locked_until` and is admitted again. The account is effectively unlocked and rate-limit-free for the next 15 minutes; only then does the CASE reset the counter and restart the cycle. Net effect: a lockout covers 50% of wall-clock time and is cyclically defeated.
- Proposed fix: Change the guard at `:95` from `!result.lockedUntil` to `!result.lockedUntil || result.lockedUntil.getTime() <= now.getTime()`, and make the reset CASE at `:68-69` compare against `now` rather than `now - LOCKOUT_DURATION_MS` so an expired lock resets the counter on the next attempt instead of persisting a stale ≥5 count.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**H-8. Semantic-relevance stale-snapshot CAS check executes on the pool client outside the transaction it guards — violates ADR 0004 and the codebase's own documented fix**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/jobs/semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:512-520` re-reads the snapshot via `getMahallaDailySnapshot(db, ...)` where `db` is the pool-backed client, then compares at `:522-543` and throws on mismatch. The write it authorises does not begin until `:559` `await withTransactionalIntake(pool, boss, async ({ tx, enqueueJob }) => {`. The check and the commit therefore run on two different PostgreSQL sessions. The identical bug was identified and fixed in `apps/backend/src/modules/topics/topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:330-336`, whose comment states verbatim that a CAS check run on `db` "can only catch a snapshot that had already advanced BEFORE the check — never the window it is advertised to catch", and `:374-401` now re-reads through `tx`. `apps/backend/src/modules/topics/jobs/topic-projection-job-handler.ts:224-257` does it correctly too, taking a `for('update')` row lock and the CAS inside the transaction.
- Failure scenario: Two messages land in the same district/mahalla/calendar day. Worker A reads snapshot revision R1 at `:512`, the check passes, and it begins the AI evaluation. Between A's check and A's `BEGIN` at `:559`, worker B commits new accepted evidence, advancing the snapshot to R2. A's transaction opens and inserts `ai_operations` (`:574-590`) and enqueues topic assignment (`:667-672`) pinned to the stale `contextRevision: initialRevision` / `snapshotFingerprint: initialFingerprint` (`:584-585`) — exactly the out-of-order commit ADR 0004 (`docs/adr/0004-optimistic-ai-concurrency-stale-rejection.md:8`) promises cannot happen. Blast radius is contained because the downstream coordinator re-verifies CAS on `tx` and throws `StaleSnapshotError` (`topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:387-401`), so the outcome is a wasted AI call and a persisted `ai_operations` row asserting provenance against a snapshot that was already superseded, not a corrupt projection.
- Proposed fix: Move the snapshot re-read and comparison from `:512-543` inside the `withTransactionalIntake` callback at `:559`, passing `tx` to `getMahallaDailySnapshot` (as `topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:375-383` already does with `tx as unknown as DbClient`), so the guard and the commit share one session.
- Confidence: Confirmed

##### Medium

**M-21. `reclassifyEvidence` performs an unguarded read-modify-write on `topics.required_derived_generation`, losing increments and silently suppressing projections**
