- Proposed fix: Keep failing closed but emit a distinct status or a dedicated metric/alert counter, and invoke `assertPrivacyBoundary` on the `resultPayload` write path as well as the read path.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**L-24. Manual-override paths pin an AI profile without filtering by operation type**
- Evidence: `topics/topic-evidence-management-service.ts:612-616` and `:1147-1151` select `aiProfiles` with `isActive` only — no `operationType` predicate — and use the resulting id as `pinnedProfileId` for a `SEMANTIC_RELEVANCE` operation row (`:630`, `:1163`). Profiles are per-operation (`adapters/db/schema/ai.ts:22`; seeds at `adapters/db/seeds.ts:31,54,77`).
- Failure scenario: The audit record for a manual promotion names a `TOPIC_MATCHING` or `TOPIC_DERIVED_PROJECTION` profile, so provenance shown in the console is wrong; if that id is ever replayed into an actual gateway call, `AiGateway.getProfile` returns a profile whose provider and model do not match the operation.
- Proposed fix: Filter with `and(eq(aiProfiles.operationType, 'SEMANTIC_RELEVANCE'), eq(aiProfiles.isActive, true))`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**L-25. Manual-override writes bypass the gateway's schema parse for `relevant_lanes`**
- Evidence: `topics/topic-evidence-management-service.ts:636-642`, `:646-652` and `:1169-1175` write `relevant_lanes: params.lanes` directly into `result_payload`, never passing through `SemanticRelevanceResultSchema` or the gateway's `safeParse`.
- Failure scenario: If the calling route does not validate `lanes` against `QualifyingLaneSchema`, a non-enum lane string can be persisted into `result_payload.relevant_lanes`, which is then read back as `QualifyingLane[]` by consumers such as `topics/topic-evidence-management-service.ts:307,491` and used in a JSONB containment query at `:202`. Whether upstream validation exists was not verified in this phase.
- Proposed fix: Parse `params.lanes` with `QualifyingLaneSchema.array().min(1)` at the service boundary before persisting.
- Confidence: Unverified hypothesis

##### AI operation inventory (Phase 3.1)

| Operation | Prompt template | Untrusted input reaches it? | Output schema-validated? | Persists what | Cost bound |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEMANTIC_RELEVANCE | system `ai/ai-config.ts:23`; user `ai/semantic-relevance-evaluator.ts:129-266` | **Yes** — candidate `:152`, burst `:139`, author prior evidence `:160`, preceding message `:254`, parent verbatim `:175`, full snapshot list `:257` via `context-snapshot.ts:204` | Yes — `SemanticRelevanceResultSchema` `:29-78`, `safeParse` at `ai-gateway.ts:236` | `result_payload` = full parsed object incl. `reasoning` + `accepted_message_ids` (`semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:587`); attempts at `:594` | **None.** `maxAttempts` 3 × `timeoutMs` 10000, × pg-boss `retryLimit` 3 |
| TOPIC_MATCHING | system `topics/topic-matching-evaluator.ts:184`; user `:~300-389` | **Yes** — candidate text, same-day topic evidence, nearest earlier message verbatim `:374,377-379` | Yes — `TopicMatchingResultSchema` `:28-134`, `safeParse` at `ai-gateway.ts:236` | `result_payload` = matching decision + `reasoning` (`topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:420`) | **None.** Same ladder |
| TOPIC_DERIVED_PROJECTION | system `topics/topic-projection-evaluator.ts:179`; user `:325-456` | **Yes** — target evidence verbatim `:386-394`, other topics' evidence `:432-444` | Yes — `TopicProjectionResultSchema` `:56-144`, plus post-generation guardrails `:487-539` | `result_payload` = `summary`, `lanes`, `anchorEvidenceId`, `isHokimRelated`, `latestUpdate` (`topic-projection-job-handler.ts:273-279`); failure path `{ error: err.message }` `:444-446` | **None.** `retryLimit` 5 × `retryDelay` 15 (`boss-client.ts:44-50`) |
| (MOCK — tests/CLI only) | n/a — `adapters/ai-providers/mock-provider-adapter.ts` echoes a configured response | n/a | Yes, via the same gateway | n/a | n/a |

##### Answers to queued open questions (Phase 3.1)

- **OQ-11 — Does the AI gateway validate model output before persisting? YES.** `ai/ai-gateway.ts:236` calls `options.schema.safeParse(parsedJson)`; on failure it throws `INVALID_OUTPUT_SEMANTICS` (`:238-248`) and retries per profile policy — it never coerces, casts or persists the raw value (re-verified by the orchestrator). All three operation schemas constrain lane fields with `QualifyingLaneSchema`: `semantic-relevance-evaluator.ts:55`, `topic-matching-evaluator.ts:102`, `topic-projection-evaluator.ts:85`. H-6's blast radius is therefore **closed for the AI path**: a model-chosen non-enum `primaryLane` cannot be persisted there. Residual gap: the manual-override writers bypass the parse (L-25, unverified at route level).
- **OQ-28 — What does `ai_operations.result_payload` actually contain? M-29 is resolved: more than sanitized metadata.** For `SEMANTIC_RELEVANCE` it is the entire validated model object including model-written `reasoning` and `accepted_message_ids`. For `TOPIC_DERIVED_PROJECTION` it holds `summary` and `latestUpdate` — free Uzbek Cyrillic prose synthesized from citizens' messages. Raw `verbatimText` is not written directly, but reaches the column by two indirect routes: a prompt-injected model can echo citizen text into `reasoning`/`summary` (C-4), and Zod enum-failure text carrying model-echoed values is written into the sibling `ai_provider_attempts.sanitized_error_message` (M-32). Both are returned to district users behind a guard that only inspects key names. The column comment at `adapters/db/schema/ai.ts:53` is inaccurate.
- **OQ-21 — Can a single AI request exceed the 10-minute queue expiry and be re-delivered while still running? Not with shipped defaults, but the bound is not structural.** The HTTP client timeout is `AbortSignal.timeout(payload.timeoutMs || 10000)` (`http-provider-adapter.ts:17,158`), `timeoutMs` from `profile.timeoutMs` (default 10000, `schema/ai.ts:29`). One gateway call is bounded by ≈ 3 × 10 s + backoff ≈ 31.5 s (`ai-gateway.ts:160-162,317-325`), far under `expireInMinutes: 10`. But `ai_profiles.timeout_ms` is operator-editable, the total is `timeoutMs × maxAttempts + backoff` with no clamp against the queue expiry, and the only aggregate budget — `options.deadlineMs` (`ai-gateway.ts:189-195`) — is never supplied by any caller. Any profile with `timeoutMs` above ~190 s lets a gateway call outlive the job expiry and be billed twice. Recorded as **M-31**.

##### Verified sound (Phase 3.1)

- **Model output is Zod-validated and rejected, never coerced**: `ai/ai-gateway.ts:236-249`; JSON-fence stripping is provider-generic and runs before the parse at `:210-213`.
- **Every AI operation constrains lanes with `QualifyingLaneSchema`**, so a model-chosen non-enum lane cannot be persisted on the AI path: `semantic-relevance-evaluator.ts:55`, `topic-matching-evaluator.ts:102`, `topic-projection-evaluator.ts:85`.
- **The gateway does not silently rewrite caller-owned fields**; per-schema tolerance is colocated with each schema via `z.preprocess`: `ai/types.ts:51-64`, `semantic-relevance-evaluator.ts:29-51`, `topic-matching-evaluator.ts:28-80`, `topic-projection-evaluator.ts:56-75`.
- **API keys are carried only in headers** (`Authorization`, `x-goog-api-key`), never in URL or body: `http-provider-adapter.ts:38,64,94`; key patterns are redacted before persistence: `ai-gateway.ts:296-300`.
- **The provider adapters log nothing** — neither prompts nor responses: no logger import or `console.*` call in `adapters/ai-providers/*.ts`, and a grep for `userPrompt|systemPrompt|rawContent` shows no logging of prompt content anywhere.
- **Snapshot assembly is district + mahalla + day scoped**, so district B's content cannot enter district A's prompt: `ai/context-snapshot.ts:78-83`. Preceding-message and intervening-count lookups are district + chat scoped: `semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:392-399,423-430`.
- **District lifecycle is checked both before and after the AI call**, failing closed: `semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:112-142,479-509`; `topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:300-326`.
- **Snapshot revision CAS runs after the AI call and throws to force a retry**: `semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:511-543`; the coordinator re-reads inside the transaction: `topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:370-402`.
- **Topic projection re-validates the model's anchor evidence against the target topic after generation**: `topic-projection-evaluator.ts:487-539`.
- **Failure telemetry never downgrades a committed projection**: `topic-projection-job-handler.ts:450-454`.

---

#### Phase 3.2 — Telegram Ingestion

Phase verdict: Webhook authenticity is genuinely enforced (constant-time, DB-authoritative tenant resolution, real intake dedup), but the ingestion boundary is unthrottled and attacker-drivable, the webhook secret is derived from the at-rest master key with a hardcoded non-production fallback, and the userbot kill switch and status setter are not enforced atomically.

**Counts: 0 Critical, 4 High, 4 Medium, 3 Low.**

> **Orchestrator verification note.** H-16, H-19 and the constant-time secret check were independently re-verified on disk by the orchestrator. The separator-loss bug recurred in this report (`src/modules` → `srcmodules`, `entrypoints/http.ts` → `entrypointshttp.ts`, `src/cli` → `srccli`, `docs/adr` → `docsadr`, `userbot/userbot-connection-manager.ts` → `userbotuserbot-connection-manager.ts`); all paths below were corrected by the orchestrator.

##### High

**H-16. Userbot bootstrap overwrites the DISABLED kill switch (TOCTOU across human-interactive network waits)**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/userbot-session/userbot-bootstrap-service.ts:76-82` (guard reads status), `:94-129` (sendCode → human types SMS code → signIn → optional 2FA prompt), `:139-152` (UPDATE sets `status: 'ACTIVE'` with `WHERE id = session.id` **only** — no status predicate; re-verified by the orchestrator at `:151`)
- Failure scenario: A Product Owner hits the kill switch (`POST .../userbot-session/disable`) while a bootstrap is in flight. The bootstrap already passed the DISABLED check at `:76`, so it holds the pre-disable snapshot in memory. The window spans sendCode, a human reading and typing an SMS code, and a possible 2FA prompt — seconds to minutes. The transaction then flips `DISABLED` → `ACTIVE` unconditionally. The documented "immediate kill switch" (ADR 0009:30) silently reverts, MTProto ingestion resumes, and the audit trail shows `USERBOT_SESSION_ACTIVATED` with no record that a DISABLED state was overridden. Same race can resurrect a session disabled for cause mid-flow.
- Proposed fix: Add a status predicate to the activating UPDATE — `.where(and(eq(id, session.id), inArray(status, ['PENDING'])))` — and treat 0 returned rows as a conflict error. Alternatively re-verify status inside the transaction with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` before the set.
- Confidence: Confirmed (re-verified by the orchestrator)

**H-17. No rate limit or ingest cap anywhere on the ingestion path; 256KB body allowed per request**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/entrypoints/http.ts:93-101` (no rate-limit plugin registered; package.json has no `@fastify/rate-limit`), `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/telegram-intake-routes.ts:32` (`bodyLimit: 262144`), `:33-49` (preHandler is secret-check only), `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/telegram-intake-service.ts:511-536` (one row per message), `:591-635` (one pg-boss job per message). Only rate limiter in the codebase is the login limiter (`modules/auth/auth-service.ts:102-107`).
- Failure scenario: Any ordinary member of any mapped group is an authorized sender — no forgery needed. A single account posting continuously produces one `telegram_intake_records` row (jsonb `raw_payload` up to 256KB), one pg-boss job, and one downstream AI evaluation per message, with no per-user, per-chat, per-district or global ceiling (H-15 confirmed still absent). Cost is linear and unbounded: 1 msg/sec × 256KB ≈ 22GB/day of jsonb, plus AI spend with no ceiling. Retention never deletes the intake rows (H-12), so the store only grows.
- Proposed fix: Register `@fastify/rate-limit` scoped to the two webhook routes keyed on `botId` + `chatId`, and add a durable per-(district, chat, user) per-minute counter check inside `ingestTelegramMessage` before the insert, rejecting over-cap with a 200 `DROPPED` so Telegram does not retry.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**H-18. Edited-message path re-enqueues on every replay with no dedup, and can defer debounce indefinitely**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/telegram-intake-service.ts:428-455` (edit branch finds existing row), `:447-455` (`processedAt` set → DROPPED, the only dedup), `:458-464` (raw_payload overwritten in place), `:466-494` (unconditional `enqueueJob` when `payload.userId` is present, `startAfter: SLIDING_DEBOUNCE_WINDOW_SECONDS`), `adapters/jobs/boss-client.ts:99-113` (queues are `standard` policy; `singletonKey` alone performs no dedup — C-3), `modules/telegram-intake/jobs/burst-debounce-job-handler.ts` (consumes the queue)
- Failure scenario: A replayed `edited_message` for a message whose `processed_at` is still null skips the DUPLICATE collapse entirely (the collapse at `:541-551` only fires on the insert path). Each replay is one more pg-boss row sharing the same `singletonKey`, which dedups nothing on a standard queue. Two consequences: (a) unbounded queue growth from a single hostile sender replaying one edit; (b) because `startAfter` is re-armed on every replay, sending one edit every 24 seconds keeps pushing the debounce job's start time forward forever, so that chat's burst never fires and its signals never reach the AI — silent loss of a mahalla's intake while the attacker sustains it. The `if (payload.userId)` gate is attacker-satisfiable: `userId` derives from `rawMsg.from?.id`, a client-supplied field.
- Proposed fix: Give the edit path the same idempotency as the insert path — add a `debounceEnqueuedAt` (or reuse `batchId`) column and only enqueue when it is null, setting it in the same UPDATE at `:458-464`; clear it when the debounce handler claims the batch. That makes N replays produce exactly one job.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**H-19. Webhook secret is HMAC of the public botId under the at-rest master key, with a hardcoded dev fallback**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/webhook-security.ts:8-14` (`deriveWebhookSecret` = `HMAC-SHA256(getEncryptionKey(), botId)`; re-verified by the orchestrator), `adapters/crypto/token-cipher.ts:17` (`FALLBACK_DEV_KEY = 'dev_encryption_key_32_bytes_len!'`), `:26-34` (returns the fallback whenever `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`), `modules/telegram-intake/telegram-intake-routes.ts:38-48` (the derived value is the whole authentication decision), `apps/backend/src/cli/register-webhook.ts:9` (default webhook host is a public ngrok URL)
- Failure scenario: Two distinct problems. (1) The same `ENCRYPTION_KEY` both encrypts every bot token and userbot session string at rest **and** derives every webhook shared secret. One key disclosure — a stray log line, a backup, an env dump — simultaneously decrypts all stored Telegram credentials and lets the attacker forge a valid `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` for any botId, i.e. full unauthenticated webhook injection. Conversely, rotating the key silently invalidates every registered webhook: the routes start returning 401 to Telegram for all bots at once, and Telegram backs off with no alert. (2) On any deployment where `NODE_ENV !== 'production'`, the key is the literal repo constant, so anyone who knows a botId — which is the webhook URL path itself — can compute the exact `secret_token` and post arbitrary forged updates, injecting verbatim text straight into the AI pipeline. The dev webhook is deliberately tunneled to the internet by ngrok (`register-webhook.ts:9,30`), so this is a reachable configuration, not a theoretical one.
- Proposed fix: Generate the webhook secret as independent random bytes at bot-registration time, store it in its own column alongside the bot row, and verify against that stored value — removing `deriveWebhookSecret` from the request path entirely. Separate the at-rest data key from any request-authentication key. If derivation must stay, key it with a dedicated `WEBHOOK_SECRET_KEY` that fails closed when unset in every environment, including development.
- Confidence: Confirmed (production impact of (2) depends on `NODE_ENV` on the VPS — see OQ-36)

##### Medium

**M-35. `updateUserbotSessionStatus` applies any status with no state-machine allowlist**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/userbot-session/userbot-session-service.ts:421-445` (`if (updates.status !== undefined) patch.status = updates.status;` — no validation against `UserbotSessionStatus`), `:13` (the union includes BANNED/DISABLED), exported publicly via `modules/userbot-session/index.ts:1`. Contrast the guarded transitions at `:319-324` (disable refuses BANNED) and `:380-382` (enable refuses BANNED).
- Failure scenario: The correct guards exist in `disableDistrictUserbotSession` and `enableDistrictUserbotSession`, but this generic setter bypasses all of them: a caller can move `BANNED` → `ACTIVE`, or `DISABLED` → `ACTIVE`, and can clear the session secret to null while leaving `status: 'ACTIVE'`, producing an ACTIVE session with no credentials. A BANNED → ACTIVE flip directly defeats the ADR 0009 ban containment (ADR 0009:28) and restarts reconnection attempts on a banned account. No caller exists in `apps/backend/src` today (only the definition), so this is latent rather than live — but it is exported from the module barrel and is exactly the API a future watchdog or worker would reach for.
- Proposed fix: Introduce a `USERBOT_SESSION_TRANSITIONS` map (PENDING→ACTIVE|DISABLED, ACTIVE→BANNED|DISABLED, DISABLED→PENDING|ACTIVE, BANNED→∅) and reject any transition not in it with `ConflictError`; also reject setting `status: 'ACTIVE'` when the session secret is absent.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**M-36. USERBOT group mappings are marked VALID with no live verification of the chat**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-groups/telegram-groups-service.ts:225-234` (create: USERBOT branch only checks that a session is ACTIVE — no Telegram call), `:454-461` (update: when the chat changed and transport is USERBOT, `chatTitle` is set to `newMahallaName` and `chatUsername` to null — no Telegram lookup), `:514` (`status: isChatChanged ? 'VALID' : group.status`), `modules/telegram-groups/telegram-groups-routes.ts:141-143` (create/update/delete are behind origin guard + Product Owner)
- Failure scenario: The BOT_API path calls `getTelegramChat` + `verifyBotGroupMembership` + `checkGroupPrivacyMode` before persisting (`:246-260`); the USERBOT path performs none of that. A Product Owner can create or re-point a mapping at any arbitrary `telegram_chat_id`, and it lands immediately `VALID` with a fabricated title. Ingestion then only needs the district's userbot session to be ACTIVE and the incoming chat id to match — so a mistyped or maliciously chosen chat id silently becomes a live intake tenant and its content is attributed to that mahalla. Authenticated-insider and misconfiguration risk rather than external attack, hence Medium.
- Proposed fix: On the USERBOT path, require the same positive verification as BOT_API — resolve the chat through the district's userbot client (or a `getChat`-equivalent on the MTProto port) and reject with `TelegramChatNotFoundError` when the account cannot see the chat, instead of synthesizing `chatTitle` from user input.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**M-37. Userbot auth client is never disconnected; a live authenticated MTProto socket and apiHash are retained on failure paths**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/adapters/telegram/userbot-auth-client.ts:50-52` (`this.client`, `this.currentApiId`, `this.currentApiHash` held as instance state), `:89` (`await client.connect()`), `:98` (`this.client = client` — connection retained after sendCode), `:103-109` and `:132-145` (failure branches throw without disconnecting), `modules/userbot-session/userbot-bootstrap-service.ts:90-136` (no `disconnect` anywhere in the flow; `authClient` is default-constructed per call at `:90` with no registry). Grep for `disconnect()` across `apps/backend/src` shows callers only in `adapters/telegram/userbot-client-adapter.ts` and `modules/userbot/userbot-connection-manager.ts` — never the auth client.
- Failure scenario: Every bootstrap attempt leaves a connected, authenticated MTProto client alive in the process, plus the plaintext `apiHash` on the instance. `createDefaultUserbotAuthClient()` returns a fresh instance each call with no handle kept by the caller, so these objects are unreachable but not closed — the sockets persist until the process exits or the peer drops them. On repeated failed attempts (wrong SMS code, wrong 2FA password) the client accumulates open connections and retains credentials in memory; a heap dump or an in-process crash reporter would expose the apiHash. The CLI entrypoint masks this because the process exits (`apps/backend/src/cli/bootstrap-userbot-session.ts:128-130` closes only the pool), but any in-process caller — the shape the port invites — leaks.
- Proposed fix: Give `UserbotAuthClientPort` a `disconnect()` and call it in a `finally` inside `bootstrapUserbotSession` around `:92-136`; null out `currentApiHash`/`currentApiId` on disconnect. Keep the browser-session isolation honest by not constructing the auth client outside the CLI.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**M-38. Second, unadvertised webhook endpoint bypasses tenant authorization entirely**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-groups/telegram-groups-routes.ts:82-138` (`POST /api/v1/telegram/webhook/:botId`, same secret-token preHandler at `:91-101`), `:121` (delegates to `handleIncomingWebhookMessage`), `modules/telegram-groups/telegram-group-engine.ts:189-251` (acceptance decided by `filterTelegramMessage` + an in-memory session keyed on `chatId`, plus a `botId` equality check at `:215`). Neither the watchdog nor the registration CLI ever registers this URL — both use `/api/v1/webhooks/telegram/:botId` (`modules/telegram-intake/telegram-watchdog.ts:79`, `src/cli/register-webhook.ts:30`).
- Failure scenario: Two live public webhook routes exist for the same bot, sharing the same derived secret, but only one runs `resolveDistrictBotAndGroup`. The test route decides "is this message from an approved, correctly-tenanted group?" from process-local memory seeded by a Product Owner clicking "start test" (`telegram-group-engine.ts:35-90`), never from `district_telegram_groups`. Any message arriving in that chat while a session is open — from any member, including a hostile one — permanently stamps the group `VALID` and sets `testMessageReceivedAt` (`:222-248`), after which it is indistinguishable from a genuine validation. The endpoint is also the only ingestion path that does not consult district status or transport, so a group whose district has since gone inactive can still be validated through it.
- Proposed fix: Retire this endpoint and route the test-session check inside `processTelegramWebhookUpdate` after authorization (the intake route already reads the chat id at `telegram-intake-routes.ts:72-75`), or add a hard `api/v1/telegram/webhook` → 410 tombstone once the intake route handles it.
- Confidence: Confirmed

##### Low

**L-26. Registration CLI prints the derived webhook secret to stdout**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/cli/register-webhook.ts:33-42` (`webhookPayload` contains `secret_token: secretToken`), `:42` (`console.log('Payload configuration:', JSON.stringify(webhookPayload, null, 2))`)
- Failure scenario: The full `setWebhook` payload, including the `secret_token` that authenticates all inbound updates for that bot, is written to stdout — and therefore into any terminal scrollback, CI log, or `tee`d deploy transcript. Combined with H-19 the printed value is stable (a pure function of the master key and botId), so a leaked log line is a durable webhook credential, not a one-time nonce.
- Proposed fix: Delete line 42; log only `{ url, allowed_updates, max_connections }`. The file also hardcodes bot id `8293431272` at `:16` and a personal ngrok host at `:9` — worth parameterizing while it is being touched.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**L-27. Bot-command filter bypass via zero-width prefix**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/telegram-content-qualification.ts:157` (`message.text.trimStart().startsWith('/')`), `:151` (entity check requires `offset === 0`), `:102-107` (`hasMeaningfulText` strips `\u200B-\u200D`, `\uFEFF`, `\u2060`, `\u00AD`, `\u200E`, `\u200F` — but `trimStart` does not)
- Failure scenario: A message whose text is `"\u200B/start"` is not a slash command by either check, and its zero-width prefix is stripped by `hasMeaningfulText`, so it qualifies as ordinary `TEXT` content and enters the AI pipeline. Practical impact is small — a stray command line gets evaluated as a civic signal — but the "no bot commands reach the AI" invariant can be broken by any sender, at will, in a loop.
- Proposed fix: Normalize before testing — run the same zero-width/format-character strip used by `hasMeaningfulText` and then check `startsWith('/')`, in both `isTelegramCommand` and `filterTelegramMessage`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

**L-28. Test-session webhook path logs the raw error object without sanitization**
- Evidence: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/telegram-intake-routes.ts:117` (`req.log.error({ err, botId, chatId }, ...)`), contrasted with the sanitized main path at `:215` (`sanitizeDriverError` from `modules/telegram-intake/webhook-security.ts:44-64`)
- Failure scenario: The main intake handler deliberately scrubs driver errors before logging; the test-session handler above it logs the untouched `err` through Fastify's Pino serializer. A `pg` error carries the failing query text and, depending on the driver, parameter detail — bypassing the AD-11 sanitization the sibling path was built to enforce. The two paths sit nine lines apart, so the sanitizer is easy to apply to one and forget on the other.
- Proposed fix: Route this log through `sanitizeDriverError` as well, matching `:215-224`.
- Confidence: Confirmed

| Bot token | `district_telegram_bots.encrypted_token`/`token_iv`/`token_tag` (`schema/district-telegram-bots.ts:15-19`) | Yes — AES-256-GCM | In URL path at `telegram-watchdog.ts:97,126,131` and `register-webhook.ts:45,47,56`; watchdog logs only `botId`/`statusCode`/Telegram `description`, never the URL. Adapter errors redacted via `telegram-client.ts:112` + `redactTokenFromUrl:36-42` |

Held in memory at `userbot-auth-client.ts:52` and `userbot-client-adapter.ts:50` |

##### Verified sound (Phase 3.2)

- **`verifyTelegramSecretToken` is genuinely constant-time and length-safe**: both sides SHA-256'd before `crypto.timingSafeEqual`, so `RangeError` is impossible and there is no early-return on prefix mismatch — `webhook-security.ts:22-38` (re-verified by the orchestrator). Both webhook routes require it, and the preHandler reply short-circuits the handler (`telegram-intake-routes.ts:41-48`).
- **Pointer 1 resolved**: the actual webhook authentication is exactly this header check; there is no separate token-in-URL or IP-allowlist check. A party who knows only the URL cannot forge an update **in production** — it needs `ENCRYPTION_KEY` (see H-19 for the non-production case).
- **Tenant authorization is fully DB-authoritative and never trusts client-supplied identity**: bot VALID, district ACTIVE/GRACE, `accessEligible`, group mapped + VALID, group district equal to bot district, transport `BOT_API` — `telegram-intake-service.ts:122-158`; symmetric checks for USERBOT at `:205-237`.
- **Transport mutual exclusivity is enforced in both directions** — `telegram-intake-service.ts:156-158` rejects a USERBOT group arriving via bot; `:235-237` rejects a BOT_API group arriving via userbot.
- **Replay of an identical update does not create a second intake row**: unique index `telegram_intakes_district_chat_msg_idx` on `(district_id, telegram_chat_id, telegram_message_id)` (`schema/telegram-intakes.ts:53-57`) matches the `onConflictDoNothing` target exactly (`telegram-intake-service.ts:529-535`), and the 0-row result collapses to `DUPLICATE` with no enqueue (`:541-551`). `accepted_evidence_district_chat_msg_idx` (`schema/accepted-evidence.ts:37-41`) provides the same guarantee downstream. There is no `update_id` dedup column, but none is needed for the replay case.
- **Pointer 3 REFUTED**: `district_telegram_groups.last_error` cannot receive a Telegram API error. Every writer sets it to `null` or a static string — `telegram-groups-service.ts:519` (always null), `telegram-group-engine.ts:162,170` (from `session.lastError`), and the only `resolveSessionFailure` caller passes the literal `'Гуруҳ ўчирилди'` (`telegram-groups-service.ts:612`). The session's `lastError` is only ever set to two hardcoded Uzbek timeout strings (`telegram-test-session-store.ts:94,113`) or that same literal (`:143`). No path carries a driver or Telegram error message into the column.
- **Bot tokens are encrypted at rest** with a dedicated masked display column and no plaintext token column — `schema/district-telegram-bots.ts:15-19`; audit metadata on bot connect is secret-free (`telegram-bot-service.ts:220-229`).
- **Group mapping mutation requires origin guard + Product Owner** (`telegram-groups-routes.ts:141-143`), and cross-district chat hijack is blocked twice: the global unique index on `telegramChatId` (`schema/district-telegram-groups.ts:39`) and the `CROSS_DISTRICT_MISMATCH` check at `telegram-intake-service.ts:146-148`.
- **Watchdog webhook health check never logs the token-bearing URL** — it logs `botId`, `statusCode`, `expectedUrl` and Telegram's `description` only (`telegram-watchdog.ts:101-123,148-153`).
- **AR-2 (ADR 0009) — RE-CONFIRMED, with weakened containment claims.** No new *transport* risk was found: account bans, `FLOOD_WAIT` and transport instability remain inherent to operating an MTProto user account and are correctly accepted as product-level risk (`docs/adr/0009-userbot-transport-abstraction-and-accepted-risks.md:26-31`). The unified-ingestion-core and passive-only-port architecture holds — both transports converge on `withTransactionalIntake` with the same immutable dedup key, and the port exposes no write methods. Caveat: AR-2 justifies accepting the ban risk partly on its stated containment ("automated ban detection transitions session status to BANNED", "kill switch ... halt MTProto consumption instantly", `:28,30`). H-16 shows the kill switch can be silently reverted by an in-flight bootstrap, and M-35 shows an exported setter that can move BANNED → ACTIVE unguarded. The risk acceptance stands, but its containment mechanisms are less reliable than the ADR asserts; fixing those two items should be treated as preserving the validity of the acceptance rather than as new scope.

---

#### Phase 3.3 — Job Queue Resilience

Phase verdict: The queue layer's plumbing is sound — pg-boss 10.4.2 is genuinely installed, the transactional `boss.send` contract that ADR 0002 and `retry-service` depend on works exactly as assumed, the cron sweeps are registered for every queue they target, and the grace-expiry sweep is idempotent under concurrency. What is not sound is the layer's failure semantics: pg-boss cannot cancel a handler it has declared expired, so an over-running job is re-delivered while its original execution is still writing to the database; and the queue-policy repair already written for one queue (proof the team hit the idempotency trap once) was never applied to the other eight.

**Counts: 0 Critical, 1 High, 2 Medium, 1 Low.**

> **Orchestrator verification note.** H-20, M-39 and M-40 were independently re-verified on disk by the orchestrator against the pinned source (`manager.js:20-33`, `:337`, `:377-386`; `plans.js:193`, `:257-264`, `:387-400`, `:566-570`, `:838-840`) and the current `boss-client.ts:15-113`. **One subagent error was corrected:** L-29 lists five `newJobCheckInterval` call sites; a repo-wide grep finds **six** — `topic-projection-job-handler.ts:486` was omitted. **OQ-23's resolution was also independently confirmed:** `plans.js:193` creates `created_on`, and no `createdon` column or compatibility view exists anywhere in the pinned source, so M-27's detector arm is dead code. The separator-loss bug recurred in this report (`srcmodules`, `srcboss.js`, `entrypointshttp.ts`, `node_modulespg-boss/package.json`, `userbotuserbot-connection-manager.ts`); all paths below were corrected against disk by the orchestrator.

**H-20. pg-boss does not cancel a handler on expiry — an expired job is retried while its original execution is still running and still writing to the database**
- Severity: High
- Evidence: In-process expiry is `resolveWithinSeconds` (`node_modules/pg-boss/src/manager.js:20-33`). It races the handler promise against a timer and, in `finally`, calls only `reject.abort()` (`:29`), which aborts the **timer** (`tools.js:21-25`), never the handler. No `AbortSignal` is passed to the callback, and every registration site uses a plain `async (jobs) => …` (`worker.ts:88-143`). The budget comes from the fetched job's own `expireInSeconds` (`manager.js:212`, `:216`); on expiry the catch at `:218-220` calls `this.fail(name, jobIds, err)`. A second, independent expiry path is the DB-side sweep `failJobsByTimeout`, predicate `state = 'active' AND (started_on + expire_in) < now()` (`plans.js:566-570`), run from `boss.maintain()` every 120 s by default (`boss.js:115-125`, `:163-165`; interval default `attorney.js:302-306`) — and `maintain()` runs in **every** process that called `boss.start()`, including the HTTP server (`http.ts:180`). Expiry budgets (`boss-client.ts:20,27,34,41,48,55,62,69,76`): 10 min for the four AI/ingest queues and `district-subscription-expiry`; 15 min for `telegram-topic-projection`, `district-live-deletion` and `district-backup-expiry`; **30 min for `telegram-topic-retention`**.
- Failure scenario: A `telegram-topic-retention` job starts the global branch at `retention-job-handler.ts:76-114`, iterating every eligible district (`:78-86`) and calling `purgeDistrictExpiredTopicsBatch` per district (`:96`). On a deployment with enough districts this exceeds 30 minutes. At 30 min pg-boss marks the job `failed` and schedules a retry (`retryLimit: 2, retryDelay: 30`, `boss-client.ts:52-53`); the original execution receives no signal and keeps purging. The retry is picked up by a second worker poll (`fetchNextJob` uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, `plans.js:506-541`) and both sweeps now run concurrently against the same topics/evidence, both executing the any-state `DELETE FROM pgboss.job` statements recorded as H-13. When the zombie execution finally exits, its `finally` at `retention-job-handler.ts:158-162` runs `clearPendingRetryFlag` for a job pg-boss already declared failed — precisely the H-11 "re-opened guard" interleaving, except here it is deterministic after every expiry rather than racy. Second interleaving, same root cause: on SIGTERM the worker waits up to 30 s for in-flight jobs then fails them (`worker.ts:231`; `index.js:195-207`; `manager.js:114-121` `failWip()`), so a deploy mid-AI-job re-delivers the job while the old process may still be inside its transaction.
- Proposed fix: Do not rely on pg-boss's race for cancellation. (a) Give every handler a per-job wall-clock budget well below its `expireInMinutes`, checked between units of work — the retention and district-lifecycle sweeps already loop per district, so a deadline check inside the loop converts a hard kill into a clean early return with a resumable cursor. (b) Re-derive `expireInMinutes` from worst observed handler duration (the 30-min retention budget is the one to measure) and treat any expiry as a paging event rather than a silent retry. (c) Move the `clearPendingRetryFlag` call out of the per-handler `finally` (H-11's fix) so a zombie completion cannot clear a flag belonging to a newer attempt.
- Confidence: Confirmed for the mechanism — `resolveWithinSeconds`, its abort of the timer only, and the absence of any cancellation channel were read directly from the pinned source. The *duration* a production retention sweep needs in order to trip the 30-min budget is unverified (see OQ-42).

**M-39. Queue-policy repair exists for exactly one queue; the other eight have no repair path and there is no boot assertion that live policy matches intent**
- Severity: Medium
- Evidence: `boss-client.ts:99-112` — only `telegram-topic-projection` gets both `createQueue(..., { policy: 'short' })` (`:103`) **and** `updateQueue(..., { policy: 'short' })` (`:104`). The other eight use a bare `createQueue`. `plans.js:234-264`: `create_queue` is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING created_on`, and returns immediately when the insert was a no-op (`:262-264`) — an existing queue keeps its stored policy. `plans.js:387-400` is the only repair path (`update_queue`, COALESCE-ing every other column so a policy-only update is safe), and `manager.js:534` defaults a new queue's policy to `standard`.
- Failure scenario: C-3's proposed fix (a) — "create the queue with `policy: 'short'`" — is a **no-op on the live database**, because `pgboss.queue` rows already exist and `create_queue` will not touch them. Anyone applying that fix and observing no behaviour change will have no signal explaining why; the projection queue's `updateQueue` line is the standing proof that the team already discovered this once and fixed it only where it had already bitten them. Any of the eight queues ever created under a different policy keeps that policy permanently, and nothing in the boot path reads `pgboss.queue` to check.
- Proposed fix: Replace the ad-hoc `createQueue`/`updateQueue` pair with one table of intended policies and a boot routine that calls `updateQueue` (not `createQueue`) for **every** queue with its intended policy, then reads `boss.getQueues()` (`manager.js:565-568`) and fails startup on any mismatch. That converts OQ-22 from a database-forensics question into a boot-time assertion.
- Confidence: Confirmed for the SQL and the idempotency semantics, read from the pinned source. The *current live* policy of each queue remains unverified — that is OQ-22, which still requires DB access.

**M-40. A missing `pgboss.queue` row makes every enqueue return `null`, and `retry-service` misreports that null as `DUPLICATE_RETRY_IN_PROGRESS`**
- Severity: Medium
- Evidence: `plans.js:812-840` — the `insertJob` statement joins its value row to the queue table with an **INNER JOIN** (`) j JOIN ${schema}.queue q ON j.name = q.name`, `:838`) and ends `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING id` (`:839-840`). With no matching queue row the SELECT yields nothing, nothing is inserted, and `RETURNING` is empty. `manager.js:377-386` then returns `null` whenever `rows.length !== 1`. `retry-service.ts:166-179` and `:306-319` both interpret a `null` job id as `DuplicateRetryInProgressError`. Two boot paths construct a boss client without ever creating queues: `cli/reconcile-retention.ts:34,46` (`createBossClient()` then `boss.start()`, no `initBossQueues` — confirmed on disk) and `issue-routes.ts:228,310` (`deps.boss || createBossClient()`).
- Failure scenario: An operator restores a database and runs the retention-reconciliation CLI before the worker has booted (or against a schema where `pgboss.queue` is empty). Every retention enqueue returns `null`. Separately, if the retry route ever falls through to its unstarted fallback boss, the operator presses Retry and receives HTTP 409 "a retry is already queued" — while nothing was enqueued and nothing ever will be. The state is at least honest (the throw rolls back before `pendingRetry` is set — `retry-service.ts:174-179` precedes the metadata update at `:213-219`), but the *diagnosis* is wrong and points the operator away from the real cause.
- Proposed fix: Call `initBossQueues(boss)` in `reconcile-retention.ts` alongside `boss.start()`, and remove the lazy `deps.boss || createBossClient()` fallback from the retry routes (fail fast on a missing dependency). In `retry-service`, distinguish the two null causes: a cheap `SELECT 1 FROM pgboss.queue WHERE name = $1` before dispatch lets the code raise a distinct configuration error instead of a duplicate error.
- Confidence: Confirmed for the SQL and return semantics (read from the pinned source). Whether the `issue-routes` fallback is reachable in production depends on the dependency wiring in `registerAllDomainRoutes`, which was not traced — flagged as OQ-43.

**L-29. `newJobCheckInterval` is not a pg-boss option — six handlers silently poll at the 2 s default instead of the apparent 50 ms**
- Severity: Low
- Evidence: **Six** registrations pass `{ newJobCheckInterval: 50 }`: `burst-debounce-job-handler.ts:353`, `qualification-job-handler.ts:175`, `semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:765`, `topic-assignment-job-handler.ts:160`, `retention-job-handler.ts:175` and `topic-projection-job-handler.ts:486` — five of them with an `as any` cast that suppresses the type error. `manager.js:190-195` reads only `pollingInterval`, `batchSize`, `includeMetadata` and `priority` from the options; `attorney.js:286-293` (`applyPollingInterval`) reads `pollingIntervalSeconds` and otherwise defaults `pollingInterval` to **2000 ms**; `checkWorkArgs` (`:111-138`) asserts only on `batchSize`/`includeMetadata`/`priority`, so the unknown key is discarded without error. `createBossClient` sets no polling override (`boss-client.ts:86-90`).
- Failure scenario: Not a correctness defect — the pipeline drains. The observable effect is that every hop of a five-stage queue chain can add up to 2 s of idle latency before a waiting job is even looked at, i.e. a burst's end-to-end latency is up to ~10 s worse than the code's apparent intent, and the `as any` casts mean nobody will discover this from the type system.
- Proposed fix: Either pass the real option (`{ pollingIntervalSeconds: … }`, floor enforced by `POLICY.MIN_POLLING_INTERVAL_MS` at `attorney.js:287`) or delete the dead key and document that the 2 s default is intended. Removing the `as any` casts is what makes the next person notice.
- Confidence: Confirmed — the option name is absent from the pinned source's option handling, and the call-site count was corrected to six by the orchestrator.

**Existing findings this phase CONFIRMS or EXTENDS (no new IDs allocated)**

- **OQ-17 — ANSWERED, and the contract holds.** With the `db: { executeSql }` override, `createJob` destructures it as `db: wrapper` (`manager.js:337`) and uses it for the only statement it issues (`const db = wrapper || this.db`, `:377`, single call at `:378`). **No statement escapes the supplied `executeSql`.** A singleton conflict is signalled as `null` via `rows.length !== 1` (`:380-386`) and `insertJob`'s `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (`plans.js:839`), so `retry-service.ts:174-179`'s reading of `null` is *correct in form* — the defect is the INNER JOIN's second null cause (M-40).
- **OQ-23 — ANSWERED from source, without DB access. M-27's detector arm is dead code.** `topic-query-engine.ts:645-657` filters on `createdon < NOW() - INTERVAL '30 seconds'` (`:655`), but pg-boss 10.4.2 creates the column as `created_on` (`plans.js:193`) and inserts it by default (`insertJob`'s column list, `:769-784`, omits it). There is no `createdon` column and no compatibility view in the pinned version (a source-wide search finds only the `queue_created_on` PL/pgSQL variable). The query therefore throws `column "createdon" does not exist` on every call and is swallowed by the bare `catch { }` at `:661-663`, so the arm has never fired. The comment at `:662` blames test environments — that attribution is wrong; the arm is not environment-dependent.
- **OQ-24 — ANSWERED: `expireDistrictGrace` is safe against concurrent sweeps.** `subscriptions-service.ts:558-609` opens a transaction, locks `districts` `FOR UPDATE` (`:565`) then `district_subscriptions` `FOR UPDATE` (`:579`), and returns early when the locked row is no longer `GRACE` (`:582-585`) or the deadline has not passed (`:587-593`). Two overlapping minute-cron sweeps serialise on the row lock and the loser no-ops. The missing `singletonKey` is therefore a non-issue.
- **OQ-38 — ANSWERED: the debounce deferral is bounded, not indefinite.** The extension branch is gated by `timeSinceLatest < SLIDING_DEBOUNCE_WINDOW_SECONDS && totalElapsed <= MAX_DEBOUNCE_BURST_CEILING_SECONDS` (`burst-debounce-job-handler.ts:121-124`, constants `= 30` and `= 60` at `:32-33`), and `firstMessageTimestamp` is carried forward on every re-arm (`:140`), including on the edit path (`telegram-intake-service.ts:484`). Once 60 s of wall clock elapse the handler flushes regardless. Each re-arm is a **new** INSERT (`manager.js:334-396` never updates an existing row), so no job's `startAfter` is mutated in place; `expireInMinutes` therefore bounds the residue, not the chain. H-18's deferral is bounded at ~60 s per contiguous burst.
- **OQ-15 (partially) — the four cron `schedule` rows are registered from code.** `boss.schedule` is called for `telegram-topic-projection-reconcile-cron` (`topic-projection-job-handler.ts:491-496`, re-verified on disk), `district-subscription-expiry-cron` (`subscription-expiry-job-handler.ts:53-58`), and `district-live-deletion-cron` + `district-backup-expiry-cron` (`district-deletion-job-handler.ts:202-207`, `:225-230`). All four queue names are created in `initBossQueues` (`boss-client.ts:105,108,110,112`), so `schedule`'s foreign-key guard (`timekeeper.js:180-188`) cannot fire. This does **not** close OQ-15: whether the worker process actually ran these registrations, and whether the rows are present live, still needs DB access.
- **H-9 confirmed, with the fix now known to be a config-only change.** The dead-letter machinery is present and functional in the pinned version — `failJobs` inserts into the DLQ whenever `state = 'failed' AND dead_letter IS NOT NULL AND NOT name = dead_letter` (`plans.js:693-705`), and the column exists (`:140`, `:197`). No queue passes `deadLetter` (`boss-client.ts:99-112`; a repo-wide search of `apps/backend/src` for `deadLetter` returns nothing). Adding a dead-letter queue and a single `deadLetter:` argument per `createQueue` is sufficient.
- **H-11 extended by H-20.** All seven `clearPendingRetryFlag`-in-`finally` sites named in Phase 2.3 are still present and unchanged (`burst-debounce-job-handler.ts:339-343`, `qualification-job-handler.ts:159-163`, `semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:747-751`, `topic-projection-job-handler.ts:468-472`, `retention-job-handler.ts:158-162`, `district-deletion-job-handler.ts:141-145` and `:177-181`). H-20 supplies the missing trigger: an expiry makes the flag-clearing zombie deterministic rather than hypothetical.
- **OQ-21 — the worst reading is confirmed, and it is broader than the AI path.** Phase 3.1 recorded M-31 (double-billing) for the AI queues' 10-minute budget. The mechanism in H-20 shows the exposure is at-least-once *concurrent execution with concurrent writes* on every queue, and the largest budget (30 min, retention) belongs to the queue that performs destructive purges.

**VERIFIED SOUND**

- **pg-boss version and queue construction.** `node_modules/pg-boss/package.json:3` is `10.4.2`. Every queue is created before use in the HTTP, worker and `reconcile-restore` entrypoints (`http.ts:180-181`, `worker.ts:168-169`, `reconcile-restore.ts:24-25`).
- **`updateQueue` cannot silently wipe a queue's retry/retention settings.** `plans.js:389-398` COALESCEs every column, so `boss-client.ts:104`'s policy-only call leaves `retry_limit`, `retry_delay`, `retry_backoff`, `expire_seconds`, `retention_minutes` and `dead_letter` untouched. The line is safe as written.
- **`singletonSeconds: 300` passes pg-boss's own assertion.** `attorney.js:102` requires `singletonSeconds <= defaults.archiveSeconds`; the default archive window is 43 200 s (`attorney.js:199`, `:204`). The two sends that rely on real dedup (`retry-service.ts:168`, `:308`) are within budget.
- **`withTransactionalIntake` transaction handling is correct.** `boss-client.ts:227-266`: `BEGIN` on a dedicated pooled client, the pgboss insert routed through the same client (`:239-251`), `COMMIT` only after the callback resolves (`:255`), `ROLLBACK` on throw (`:259`), and the connection is destroyed rather than reused when the rollback itself failed (`:261,265`). No handler performs an external network call inside the transaction — the AI inference in `semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts` completes before `withTransactionalIntake` opens at `:559`, and `telegram-intake-service.ts:568` calls a pure function.
- **`fetchNextJob` serialises correctly under `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`** (`plans.js:506-541`), so no two workers can claim the same job row — the concurrency exposure in H-20 comes from the *same job being re-created after expiry*, not from a double-claim.
- **Job-state ordering is internally consistent.** `JOB_STATES` is declared in ascending order (`plans.js:5-12`) and the enum is created in the same order with the comment explaining why (`plans.js:104-117`), so the `state < 'active'` and `state <> 'failed'` predicates used by fetch, purge and archive behave as intended.
- **Cron scheduling is not silently disabled.** `timekeeper.js:44-48` aborts when `archiveSeconds < 60`; the default is 43 200 (`attorney.js:199`), and `createBossClient` overrides nothing (`boss-client.ts:86-90`). `schedule` defaults to true (`attorney.js:159`).
- **Cron fan-out is safe across processes.** `timekeeper.js:119-139` gates every tick behind `trySetCronTime` (`plans.js:380-384`), so the HTTP server and the worker both calling `boss.start()` cannot double-fire a schedule, and the maintenance sweep is additionally advisory-locked (`plans.js:961-980`).
- **The three recovery sweeps read only bounded batches and cannot stall.** `processOverdueGraceSubscriptions` (`subscriptions-service.ts:810-842`), `processOverdueBackupExpiries` (`district-deletion-service.ts:708-750`) and `processOverdueCancelledDistricts` (`:752-793`) each `.limit(100)` with per-item catch-and-log, so a >100 backlog drains slowly rather than wedging. (Confirms the Phase 2.2 pointer; the residual risk is drain-rate, not correctness.)
- **`boss.stop` is correctly bounded and destructive-safe.** Graceful shutdown waits up to the timeout for in-flight work and then fails WIP explicitly (`index.js:152-207`, `manager.js:114-121`); `worker.ts:231` passes `{ graceful: true, timeout: 30000 }`.
- **The health probe does not crash the process on a queue failure.** The 2 s timeout race (`health-checker.ts:236-249`) and the outer catch (`:322-338`) both produce an `Unavailable` observation rather than propagating.
- **REFUTED — the lazy `createBossClient()` fallback does not leak a connection pool.** `db.js:17-21` creates the `pg.Pool` only inside `open()`, which `index.js:116-118` calls only from `start()`. A `new PgBoss(...)` that is never started (`issue-routes.ts:228,310`) allocates no pool. The defect there is M-40's null-return, not a leak.
- **REFUTED — the userbot `sessionHash -> districtId` pointer is not a queue defect.** `userbot-connection-manager.ts:58-59,202-211` is a correctly-ordered single-main-session guard: the duplicate check (`:204-211`) runs before `activeSessionKeys.set` (`:232`), and it fails closed by refusing the connection with an error log. It has no queue interaction and is not a defect at this layer.

---

#### Phase 3.4 — Storage & Crypto Adapters

Phase verdict: The cryptographic primitives are the healthiest code the audit has examined — AES-256-GCM with a fresh random 12-byte IV per operation and the tag verified before plaintext is returned, a strict 32-byte key resolver that refuses to fall back in production, Argon2id configured sensibly, CSPRNG temporary passwords, and a genuinely constant-time webhook comparison. Every defect in this phase sits at the adapter-lifecycle and file-backed-storage edges, not in the crypto. Two of them are build/runtime blockers found by exhaustively resolving every relative import in the backend rather than by reading for bugs: a module imported by three production files does not exist anywhere in the repo (H-21), and GramJS — the entire dependency ADR 0009's userbot transport rests on — is not declared, not locked and not installed (H-22). The tombstone store is a well-built atomic writer whose only flaw is a process-scoped lock (M-41), and it persists operator free-text outside the database (M-42).

**Counts: 0 Critical, 2 High, 2 Medium, 2 Low.** (Both High - H-21 and H-22 - have since been RESOLVED; see their entries and the status header.) **[SUPERSEDED — 2026-10-01.** Both H-21 and H-22 are **OPEN** by final adjudication (see their FINAL ADJUDICATION blocks at lines 1093 and 1111): the backup adapter module is absent from this checkout, and `telegram` is absent from `apps/backend/package.json`. The "have since been RESOLVED" wording above is false and is retained for traceability only.]**

> **Orchestrator verification note.** H-21, H-22, M-41, M-42, L-30 and the crypto VERIFIED SOUND block were independently re-verified on disk. H-21: `glob apps/backend/src/adapters/**/*.ts` returns no `backup/` path at all, while the three production importers and the test importer were confirmed by grep — the module is genuinely absent. H-22: `apps/backend/package.json:26-47` contains no `telegram` entry, and the only `import('telegram…')` sites are the two adapters' dynamic loads, which throw a hand-written "not installed" error. M-41: `deploy/compose/docker-compose.prod.yml:38-40` and `:74-76` mount the identical `../backup:/app/deploy/backup` on `backend` **and** `worker`, both with `TOMBSTONE_STORE_PATH=/app/deploy/backup/tombstones.json`, so the process-scoped `writeLock` (`external-tombstone-store.ts:49`) genuinely cannot serialize them. M-42: `district-deletion-service.ts:236` does source `cancellationReason` from `lockedSub.internal_note` (the `FOR UPDATE` select at `:198`), so the sanitizer's claim at `external-tombstone-store.ts:15-18` that "private notes never cross into the external tombstone store" is falsified by the field it whitelists at `:28`. **One subagent error was corrected:** the report claims `district-deletion-job-handler.ts:177-181` is a *new seventh* `clearPendingRetryFlag`-in-`finally` site extending H-11. It is **not new** — H-11 already names that exact call site (`:141-145` and `:177-181`). A repo-wide grep finds 8 call sites, all already recorded; no extension is warranted. Separator corruption did not occur this phase; all paths resolved to their true form.

**H-21. `adapters/backup/system-backup-verifier.ts` does not exist, but three production files import it — the backend cannot build or boot**
- Severity: High
- Evidence: Three production files import a module that is verifiably absent: `worker.ts:32` (import) and `:183` (`new SystemBackupRetentionVerifier()` at startup); `district-deletion-job-handler.ts:20` (import), `:158` and `:235` (instantiation); `subscriptions-routes.ts:50` (import), `:492` (instantiation). Test importer: `tests/district-backup-expiry.test.ts:25`, `:407`, `:432`. Absence confirmed four independent ways: the file and the directory both fail `Test-Path`; `glob apps/backend/src/adapters/**/*.ts` returns only `ai-providers/`, `crypto/`, `db/`, `jobs/`, `storage/`, `telegram/` — no `backup/`; `git ls-files` does not track it; `git status --short` shows only the two untracked docs, so it is not a deleted-but-dirty file. A relative import of a non-existent file is an unconditional TS2307, so `pnpm build` (`tsc`) fails and `worker.ts`/`http.ts` cannot load at runtime — worker registration constructs the verifier at startup (`worker.ts:182-183`). The scan was exhaustive: every relative `from './x.js'` import in `apps/backend/src` was resolved by mapping `.js` → `.ts`/`.tsx`/`index.ts`, and exactly three import sites are unresolvable, all this one module.

If the module were somehow stubbed at runtime, the entire backup-expiry verification subsystem — including the 5-minute cron fallback at `district-deletion-job-handler.ts:225-230` — is non-functional in this checkout, because the verifier it depends on has never existed here. Backup-expiry deadlines would never be verified, so a district's protected backups would never be confirmed expired.

Whichever is chosen, the `backup/` directory must exist before the next build. Note `Dockerfile:32` already installs the `pgbackrest` binary, so the intent is there and only the module is missing.
- Confidence: Confirmed for the absence and the import sites (verified on disk by the orchestrator). The compile failure is stated as a direct consequence of NodeNext resolution against a verified-absent file, not as an observed build result — no build was run (the audit is read-only).

All three production import sites and the test import sites now resolve. No full backend build or boot was run, so the compile-failure consequence above remains unexercised rather than disproven. **[FALSE — 2026-10-01.** The module was never landed and never committed: `apps/backend/src/adapters/backup/` does not exist; a glob of `apps/backend/src/adapters/**/*.ts` returns 36 files with no `backup/` path; `git ls-files apps/backend/src/adapters/backup` returns 0 lines and `git log --all -- '*backup-verifier*'` returns 0 lines at HEAD `5d279a7` (control: 37 tracked files under `apps/backend/src/adapters`).
