##### The structural subject — CONFIRMED, and already M-45

**The claim holds exactly.** A grep for `response\s*:\s*\{` across `apps/backend/src/**/*.ts` returns **five** matches, all in one file: `modules/health/health-routes.ts:147`, `:168`, `:215`, `:256`, `:280` (verified at `:147`, `:168`, `:215` and `:256` by reading the file; `:280` confirmed by the same grep). Those five are the five schemas M-45 already itemises at backlog line 1309. Meanwhile the contracts package is imported in **62** places across the backend, and `health-routes.ts:5-11` is the only import site whose symbols feed a `response:` key — `LivenessProbeResponseSchema`, `ReadinessProbeResponseSchema`, `PublicHealthSummaryResponseSchema`, `OverallSystemHealthResponseSchema`, `DistrictHealthResponseSchema`. `packages/api-contracts/src/index.ts` barrels 17 schema modules with no `response`-schema consumer anywhere in the backend.

**Disposition: NOT A DEFECT — already M-45.** This is an independent re-derivation on the current revision, not a new finding. M-45's evidence, mechanism and proposed fix stand as written and nothing here extends them; no ID is consumed. The re-derivation is recorded because it confirms M-45's structural premise without relying on M-45's own count.

##### Confirmed defects

**M-53. The semantic-relevance job handler logs unscrubbed provider error text, including the provider's raw error body (answers OQ-33)**

- Severity: Medium
- Evidence (OBSERVED): `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/jobs/semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:739-745` calls `console.error(JSON.stringify({ event: 'TELEGRAM_SEMANTIC_RELEVANCE_ERROR', intakeId, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) }))` — `:743` is the `err.message` read — immediately before `throw err` at `:746`. The thrown value is commonly an `AiGatewayError`: `modules/ai/ai-gateway.ts:332-338` rethrows `lastError` when it is one of that class, and the class is defined at `modules/ai/types.ts:17-38`. Its messages are built from the provider's **response body**: `adapters/ai-providers/http-provider-adapter.ts:221` interpolates `${errorBody}` into the `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` message, and `:231`, `:253`, `:261` do the same for the authentication, refusal and server-error paths. The persisted copy of that same string **is** scrubbed — `ai-gateway.ts:295-300` strips `sk-…`, `AIza…` and `Bearer …` and truncates to 200 characters — but the scrub is applied only to the `sanitizedErrorMessage` pushed into `recordedAttempts` at `:302-310`. The handler's `console.error` sits outside that path and reads the original `err.message`.
- Mechanism: two copies of one error string, one scrubbed and one not. The scrub lives in the gateway, the log call lives in the handler, and nothing connects them. The handler is registered into the worker pipeline at `entrypoints/worker.ts:96-104`, so this is a production log path, not a test-only one.
- Failure scenario: An AI provider returns a 401 or 429 whose body echoes the submitted `Authorization` or `x-goog-api-key` header — the risk OQ-34 already names. The gateway scrubs the copy written to `ai_operations`, so the database looks clean, while the handler writes the untouched body to stdout: into the container log, into whatever ships it, and into any `docker logs` transcript an operator keeps. An incident check that inspects only persisted data reports clean while the leak sits in the log.
- Severity note: Medium, not High. The leak is conditional on a provider choosing to echo credentials in an error body, which OQ-34 records as unestablished. The mechanism is confirmed; the blast radius depends on that one unknown.
- Proposed fix: Export the scrub already implemented at `ai-gateway.ts:296-300` as a shared `scrubProviderErrorText(raw: string): string`, and route the handler's `:743` through it. Do not duplicate the three regexes — the defect is precisely that one copy exists and the other call site does not use it.
- Confidence: Confirmed for the call site, the message construction and the scrub's location (all read on disk). Whether a real provider body ever carries a credential is UNKNOWN, and is OQ-34's question rather than this finding's.

**L-39. `reconcile-restore.ts:29` passes `role: 'SYSTEM'`, which is not a member of the contract's `ActorRoleSchema`, through a local structural type that bypasses the contract**

- Severity: Low
- Evidence (OBSERVED): `apps/backend/src/entrypoints/reconcile-restore.ts:29` passes `actor: { id: null, role: 'SYSTEM' }` into `reconcileDisasterRestore` at `:27-30`. The contract's authenticated-actor role is `ActorRoleSchema = z.enum(['PRODUCT_OWNER', 'DISTRICT_HOKIM'])` at `packages/api-contracts/src/auth.ts:3`, consumed by `ActorContextSchema` at `:6-13` with `role: ActorRoleSchema` at `:8`. `'SYSTEM'` is **not** in that enum. It **is** in a different, audit-specific enum: `packages/api-contracts/src/audit.ts:18-22` (`AuditActorRoleEnumSchema`, listing `'SYSTEM'` at `:21`). The parameter that receives the value uses neither contract type — `apps/backend/src/modules/retention/restore-reconciliation.ts:38` declares `actor?: { id?: string | null; role?: string | null }`, an inline structural duplicate whose `role` is a bare `string`.
- Mechanism: this is not a runtime break and does not produce a wrong value. The drift is that a structural type re-declares a contract shape more loosely than the contract, so the compiler cannot reject a role the contract forbids. The value is then consumed twice, at `restore-reconciliation.ts:562-563` (success event) and `:677-678` (failure event), each a cast — `(options?.actor?.role as 'PRODUCT_OWNER' | 'SYSTEM' | null) || 'SYSTEM'` — which is a second place the mismatch is silenced. Downstream is permissive: `modules/audit/audit-service.ts:141` types `actorRole?: string | null` and the column is free text (`adapters/db/schema/audit.ts:12`), so `'SYSTEM'` lands intact. The audit contract accepts it; the auth contract does not.
- Failure scenario: A future refactor that replaces the inline `{ role?: string }` with the real `ActorContext` — the obvious cleanup, and the one this drift invites — fails to compile at `reconcile-restore.ts:29` for a reason unrelated to what the refactor intended, and the tempting resolution is to widen `ActorRoleSchema`. That would push `'SYSTEM'` into the authenticated-actor union app-wide, weakening every route that switches on role. The drift is a trap laid for the next person, not a live fault.
- Proposed fix: Type the parameter at `restore-reconciliation.ts:38` with the audit contract's role (`AuditActorRole`, `audit.ts:23`) rather than a bare `string`, and drop the two casts at `:563` and `:678`. That states the real invariant — this is a system-actor audit path, not an authenticated request — instead of hiding it behind a structural type and a cast.
- Confidence: Confirmed for the mismatch, both enums and both casts (all read on disk). The severity rests on the refactor-trap argument, which is INFERRED.

**L-40. The debug-payload purge preserves a key the writer never sets, so an exclusion reason is destroyed at purge time**

- Severity: Low
- Evidence (OBSERVED): `apps/backend/src/modules/retention/debug-payload-retention.ts:24-29` rebuilds `raw_payload` from exactly four keys — `status`, `exclusionReason`, `reasoning`, `purgedAt` — and its docstring at `:10-12` states its purpose as purging verbatimText and debug payloads while preserving "the status and exclusionReason metadata". The writer it exists to preserve disagrees on the key name: `apps/backend/src/modules/topics/topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:711-715` writes `{ status: 'EXCLUDED', reason: 'UNASSIGNABLE_VAGUE', purgedAt: … }` — the key is **`reason`**, not `exclusionReason`. The reader expects the contract's name: `packages/api-contracts/src/signals.ts:25` declares the wire field as `exclusionReason: z.string().nullable()`, and `modules/topics/topic-evidence-management-service.ts:255-258` reads `(aiResult.exclusion_reason) || (rawPayload.exclusionReason) || null`. Two other writers do use the contract name — `telegram-intake/jobs/qualification-job-handler.ts:128` and `telegram-intake/jobs/burst-debounce-job-handler.ts:253` both write `exclusionReason` — so the coordinator is the outlier, not the rule.
- Mechanism: `debug-payload-retention.ts:26` extracts `raw_payload->>'exclusionReason'`. For a row written by the coordinator that expression is SQL `NULL`, so the rebuilt object omits the key while dropping every other key except `status`, `reasoning` and the new `purgedAt`. The one value the purge exists to keep is the one it discards, and it discards it silently.
- Failure scenario: A citizen message is classified `UNASSIGNABLE_VAGUE` and the intake row is rewritten at `topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:708-718`. Fourteen days later the sweep runs. Before the sweep an operator view can still recover a reason through the fallback chain at `topic-evidence-management-service.ts:255-258`; after it, both sources read `undefined` and `exclusionReason` goes `null` on the wire (`signals.ts:25`). The signal's history keeps the fact of exclusion and loses its cause, and the value is unrecoverable because the same statement also removed `verbatimText`.
- Severity note: Low. The lost value is one of a small fixed set of reason codes and the loss is cosmetic to the operator view, not a data-integrity or security fault. It is recorded rather than dismissed because the purge's own stated contract is violated and the mismatch is a genuine cross-module key-name drift — the exact class of defect this phase exists to find.
- Proposed fix: Align the coordinator on the contract name — change `reason:` to `exclusionReason:` at `topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:713`. Do not add a second key-preservation arm to the purge; that would entrench two names for one field. Then re-run the coordinator exclusion test, since `signal-management-crud.test.ts` asserts on this path.
- Confidence: Confirmed for the writer, the reader, the contract field and the purge's key list (all read on disk). Whether any already-purged production row lost a reason this way needs database access — see the UNVERIFIABLE list.

##### NOT A DEFECT — dismissed, with the dismissing evidence

These are the in-scope reconciliation items whose underlying defect either does not exist or is already owned by a recorded finding. Each is adjudicated; none is silently dropped.

- **Item 1 — `operational_issues` retry fields have no corresponding columns. NOT A DEFECT.** The absence is deliberate and the fields are derived at read time, exactly as the Phase 2.1 bullet suspected. `adapters/db/schema/operational-issues.ts` declares its columns through `:17-37` and no retry column exists — a grep of that file for `retryCount|nextRetryAt|lastError|attempt` returns **no matches**. The values live inside the `metadata jsonb` column (`:32`), written at `modules/issues/retry-service.ts:205-211` (`pendingRetry`, `lastRetryAt`, `retryTrackingId`, `retryCount`) and read back at `modules/issues/issue-evaluator.ts:267-274`, which pulls `pendingRetry`, `retryCount` and `lastRetryAt` out of `row.metadata` and types each before use. The contract agrees: `packages/api-contracts/src/issues.ts:94-96` declares all three `.optional()`, while `:93` declares `isRetryEligible` as required — a *computed* field, which is only coherent if the others are derived too. Contract, schema and code are consistent.
- **Items 7 and 11 — ADR 0002 non-transactional enqueue departures and the `null`-on-conflict cause. NOT A NEW DEFECT — already H-8 and M-40.** `docs/adr/0002-postgresql-pgboss-transactional-intake.md` is 19 lines of prose with no numbered normative rules; `:8` states the transactional-intake intent and `:12-19` list considered options and consequences. There is no rule text for the code to contradict, so the departures are departures from the ADR's narrative intent, which is what H-8 already records. Item 11's residual — that the ADR should name the missing-`pgboss.queue` cause so a reader does not read `null` as "duplicate" — is verbatim M-40's own proposed fix (backlog line 1028, "distinguish the two null causes"). Nothing is left for this phase.
- **Item 8 — ADR 0004's stale-rejection promise. NOT A NEW DEFECT — already H-8 and H-10.** `docs/adr/0004-optimistic-ai-concurrency-stale-rejection.md:8` does promise that a stale result is "rejected as stale, and processing is automatically re-enqueued with the new evidence snapshot", and `:17` promises summaries "never partial or out-of-order data". Both promises are wider than the code. That gap is already filed: backlog line 611 cites this ADR's `:8` by line number as H-8's evidence, and H-10 owns the missing re-enqueue on two of the three AI paths. No new ID.
- **Item 12 — AR-2 containment text (ADR 0009:28,30). NOT A DEFECT.** The ADR lines say exactly what AR-2's narrative says they say, verified by reading them: `docs/adr/0009-userbot-transport-abstraction-and-accepted-risks.md:28` promises automated ban detection "transitions session status to `BANNED`" and `:30` describes the Product Owner kill switch that halts MTProto consumption. The text is accurate and the code matches the text, so there is no contract drift here. The separate question of whether those mechanisms are *reachable* is the subject of the document correction below, not a code finding.
- **Item 13 — `docs/adr/` says nothing about tombstone expiry. NOT A NEW DEFECT — already M-30.** Independently re-verified: a grep of `docs/adr` for `tombstone` over `*.md` returns **no matches**, so no ADR states whether `district_deletion_records` is a permanent ledger. That absence is precisely M-30's proposed fix (backlog line 741, "say so in ADR 0006/0011"). This phase confirms the absence and adds nothing.
- **Item 16 — `register-webhook.ts:9,16` ngrok default and hardcoded bot id. NOT A NEW DEFECT — already F-14.** Verified on disk at the cited lines: `:9` reads `const ngrokUrl = process.argv[2] || 'https://mulled-revivable-satirical.ngrok-free.dev';` and `:16` reads `['8293431272'],`. Both are exactly as F-14 describes (backlog line 301), including its fix text ("make both required arguments; delete the literals"). The same two lines are cited a second time at backlog line 943. No new ID.
- **Item 17 — contracts unwired as Fastify response schemas. NOT A NEW DEFECT — already M-45.** See the structural-subject block above: the count, the file and the absence all reproduce M-45 exactly.
- **Item 14, the `worker.ts` half — NOT A DEFECT for `worker.ts`.** A grep of `apps/backend/src/entrypoints/worker.ts` for `AiGatewayError` returns **no matches**, and a grep for `AiGatewayError|\.message` also returns **no matches**. The file's error paths pass whole objects (e.g. `:165` `console.error('[worker:pg-boss] Background queue error:', error)`) and never read `.message`. The pointer's premise about that file is false. The real defect it was reaching for lives one module away and is recorded as **M-53** above.

##### UNVERIFIABLE

- **Item 10 — `cli/reconcile-retention.ts:34,46` as the M-40 trigger, runbook half. UNVERIFIABLE — needs the deploy runbook set and VPS access.** The code half is confirmed on disk and is already M-40: `:34` calls `createBossClient()` and `:46` calls `await boss.start()`, and the file imports only `createBossClient` (`:2`) and `reconcileRestoredRetention` (`:3`) — no `initBossQueues` import or call exists anywhere in the 73 lines read in full. The pointer's residual — "if any runbook instructs operators to run this CLI against a restored database, the runbook needs the same fix" — cannot be settled from this repository, because `deploy/backup/` does not exist here (that absence is M-47) and no reachable runbook under `deploy/` names this CLI. Access needed: the production runbook set plus `/opt/mahalla-ovozi` on the VPS, to see whether an operator is ever instructed to run this CLI before a worker has booted. This does not weaken M-40, which stands on the code alone.

##### DOCUMENT INTEGRITY CORRECTION — the AR-2 "CONTESTED PREMISE" note is false on disk

**No finding ID is consumed by this block. It corrects a document claim, not the system.**

The note dated 2026-09-30 — at backlog line 1838 at the time this correction was written, having been at line 1734 before the Phase 6.1 section was inserted — "CONTESTED PREMISE — recorded 2026-09-30 (AR-2 / H-22 contradiction)" — asserts that `apps/backend/package.json:38` reads `"telegram": "2.26.22"` and that this contradicts H-22. **It does not.** The file is **48 lines**; line 38 reads `"zod": "catalog:"`; and a grep of the whole file for `telegram` returns **zero matches**. The declared dependencies occupy `:26-39` and the devDependencies `:40-47`, so line 38 lies inside the range H-22 cites — and that range contains no `telegram` entry at all. (The dependencies block in full: `@fastify/compress`, `@fastify/cookie`, `@fastify/cors`, `@mahalla-ovozi/api-contracts`, `argon2`, `dotenv`, `drizzle-orm`, `fastify`, `fastify-type-provider-zod`, `pg`, `pg-boss`, `zod`.)

The consequence is the reverse of what the note claims. H-22's original evidence is **correct**: GramJS is not a declared dependency. The AR-2 containment narrative at line 1732 is therefore **not contested** — it stands as written, because the mechanisms it calls unreachable genuinely cannot fire against a connection that is never established.

Two things follow. First, the "CONTESTED PREMISE" note should be **struck or reversed**, and the same false premise re-checked wherever it propagated — the status header at line 3 and the AR-2 entry at line 1732 both lean on it. Second, and more seriously, this is the first false state-claim found in the *correction* layer rather than in the original audit: a note written to repair the record introduced an error of exactly the kind it existed to catch. The claim was falsifiable by one `Get-Content` of a 48-line file, and that check was not performed. It is recorded here because a correction layer that is itself unreliable is worse than no correction layer — a later reader has no way to know which of the two contradicting statements to trust. **[ACTION TAKEN 2026-10-01: the note has been REVERSED in place.** A dated correction now precedes the CONTESTED PREMISE note at the AR-2 entry; the same false premise is corrected at the Phase 4.2 bullet and at the pre-execution 6.1 stub; and rows 2 and 12 of the Layer 6 reconciliation table now record the adjudication instead of pointing at the note. No place in the document now asserts that the manifest declares `telegram`.]**

##### Open Question raised by this phase

- **OQ-52.** `clearPendingRetryFlag` has **eight** production call sites, not the one that ADR 0010 asserts. The ADR states at `:19` that "Its only production caller is `topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:780`" and repeats the single-site scope at `:70` ("`topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:778-782` must stop clearing the flag. This is the concrete code change this ADR authorises"). Disk shows eight: `topic-assignment-coordinator.ts:780`, `topics/jobs/topic-projection-job-handler.ts:470`, `telegram-intake/jobs/qualification-job-handler.ts:161`, `telegram-intake/jobs/burst-debounce-job-handler.ts:341`, `ai/jobs/semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:749`, `retention/jobs/retention-job-handler.ts:160`, and `subscriptions/jobs/district-deletion-job-handler.ts:143` and `:179` (a grep of `apps/backend` for `clearPendingRetryFlag` returns 19 matches; the eight above are the non-test call sites). **The open question is which of those eight sit on a retry-dispatch path** — i.e. which receive a job whose `data.issueId` was populated by `retry-evaluator.ts` as `payload: { …issue.metadata, issueId: issue.id }` — because that set, not the coordinator alone, is H-11's true fix scope. This phase did not trace `issueId` provenance per handler. It matters because ADR 0010's acceptance criterion at `:73` ("assert `pendingRetry` is still `true` after that failure") would pass with only the coordinator changed while the other handlers still cleared the flag on the failure path. Access needed: none beyond reading the seven remaining handlers' `issueId` provenance — this is a code-reading task, not an access-limited one, and is left open only because it belongs to H-11's fix, not to this phase's adjudication.

**IDs consumed by this phase: M-53, L-39, L-40, OQ-52.** `C-5` and `H-26` are **not** consumed — the phase found no Critical and no High. The authoritative next-free set becomes **`C-5 / H-26 / M-54 / L-41, OQ-53`**.

#### Phase 6.2 — Config, env & timezone

> **Execution record, added 2026-10-01.** The stub of the same name above remains as the record that no execution section existed before this date. This section is the executed phase.

Phase verdict: **executed 2026-10-01. Zero Critical, zero High, zero Medium, two Low. No Open Question.** The config and timezone surface is substantially sound, and both defects found are Low-severity drift: one duplicated timezone constant and one undocumented environment variable. Every ADR-scoped item in this phase's tag set was already adjudicated by Phase 6.1 or is owned by a recorded finding, so no new Medium was justified and none was manufactured.

**Counts: 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 2 Low.**

**Method.** Read-only. One agent. No build, no typecheck, no test run, no database access, no git write. Every path was re-derived with glob/grep; no line number was copied from this document. Read in full: `packages/api-contracts/src/timezone.ts` (33 lines), `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/timezone-util.ts` (107), `docs/adr/0003-same-day-calendar-boundary.md` (19), `deploy/compose/.env.example` (17), `deploy/compose/docker-compose.prod.yml` (136), `apps/backend/src/adapters/crypto/token-cipher.ts` (120), `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/ai-operation-query-service.ts` (191). Read in cited ranges: `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/context-snapshot.ts`, `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/jobs/semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts`, `apps/backend/src/adapters/db/seeds.ts`, `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/ai-config.ts`, `apps/backend/src/entrypoints/http.ts`, `apps/backend/src/modules/auth/origin-guard.ts`, `apps/backend/src/modules/topics/topic-evidence-service.ts`, `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/telegram-watchdog.ts`, `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/types.ts`, `apps/backend/src/entrypoints/worker.ts`.

##### Confirmed defects

**L-41. The Tashkent UTC offset is re-derived inline as a magic literal instead of using the exported constant**

- Severity: Low
- Evidence (OBSERVED): `apps/backend/src/modules/topics/topic-evidence-service.ts:95` reads `const adjusted = new Date(date.getTime() + 5 * 3600 * 1000);`. The canonical constant exists and is exported: `packages/api-contracts/src/timezone.ts:1-2` declares `TASHKENT_OFFSET_SECONDS = 5 * 3600` and `TASHKENT_OFFSET_MS = TASHKENT_OFFSET_SECONDS * 1000`, and `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/timezone-util.ts:9` re-exports all three symbols. Two other arithmetic sites in the same codebase consume the shared constant rather than re-deriving it — `timezone-util.ts:26` and `:28` both multiply `TASHKENT_OFFSET_SECONDS * 1000` to derive the day boundaries. The comment immediately above the literal, at `:88-89`, states the intent as "Uses deterministic UTC+5 arithmetic to avoid environment or timezone differences" — the intent and the value are both correct; only the provenance drifts.
- Mechanism: the offset is a domain constant with a single declared home, and one of the three arithmetic sites bypasses it. `5 * 3600 * 1000` is numerically identical to `TASHKENT_OFFSET_MS`, so nothing is broken today. The drift is that a change to the declared offset — or a second offset for a future region — would update the contract and `timezone-util.ts` while silently leaving this formatter on the old value, producing timestamps offset in one view and correct everywhere else.
- Failure scenario: A maintenance change moves the system to a second region, or a comment-driven edit corrects the constant at `timezone.ts:1`. The dashboard timestamps produced by this function disagree with the calendar-day bucketing produced by the constant-consuming paths. Because the offset here is applied to a display formatter rather than to a query predicate, nothing fails — the two views simply disagree, and the discrepancy is a time-of-day artefact most visible near midnight.
- Severity note: Low. No live incorrectness, and the affected surface is a display formatter. Recorded because it is a genuine single-source-of-truth violation of exactly the class this phase exists to find, and the fix is one import.
- Proposed fix: Import `TASHKENT_OFFSET_MS` from `@mahalla-ovozi/api-contracts` in `topic-evidence-service.ts` and replace `5 * 3600 * 1000` with it. Consider a lint rule or test that fails on a bare `3600` literal under `modules/`, since this is the second time the offset has been re-typed.
- Confidence: Confirmed (both files read on disk; the two constant-consuming sites confirmed by grep).

**L-42. `APP_BASE_URL` is read by the webhook watchdog but is absent from the environment template, which hardcodes a production literal as the fallback**

- Severity: Low
- Evidence (OBSERVED): `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-intake/telegram-watchdog.ts:64` reads `const baseUrl = deps.baseUrl || process.env.APP_BASE_URL || 'https://mahalla-ovozi.uz';`. The template an operator copies to `.env` does not mention it: `deploy/compose/.env.example` is 17 lines and a grep of it for `APP_BASE_URL` returns **no matches** — it documents `APP_ORIGIN` (`:8`), `SESSION_COOKIE_NAME` (`:9`), `AI_PROVIDER` (`:12`), `AI_MODEL_ID` (`:13`) and `GROQ_API_KEY` (`:14`), but not this one. A grep of `apps/backend/src` for `APP_BASE_URL` returns exactly one match — this line. The watchdog is live, not dormant: `apps/backend/src/entrypoints/worker.ts:199-216` registers `runWatchdog`, which calls `checkAndHealTelegramWebhooks` every 60 s. For contrast, the sibling variable `APP_ORIGIN` is both documented and read twice (`entrypoints/http.ts:134`, `modules/auth/origin-guard.ts:73`).
- Mechanism: an environment variable read at runtime has no entry in the artifact that enumerates the environment. The fallback literal happens to equal the documented production domain, so the documented deployment is correct by coincidence of the default rather than by configuration. Nothing requires the operator to set it and nothing warns when it is unset.
- Failure scenario: The application is deployed under a different hostname — a staging stack, a regional subdomain, or a domain migration — and the operator copies `.env.example` faithfully. `APP_ORIGIN` is set correctly, the SPA works, CORS passes, and every health probe is green; but the once-a-minute watchdog silently re-registers every bot's webhook to `https://mahalla-ovozi.uz/api/v1/webhooks/telegram/<botId>`. Telegram then delivers updates to the production host while the operator watches the new deployment receive nothing. Because the watchdog *repoints* webhooks rather than merely checking them, this is an active redirection, not a passive misconfiguration.
- Severity note: Low, deliberately not Medium. In the single documented production deployment the literal is correct, so the defect does not fire; it is a latent divergence whose trigger is a deployment change. Recorded rather than dismissed because a hardcoded literal silently overrides an unset variable — the fail-to-a-default shape this audit flags elsewhere.
- Proposed fix: Add `APP_BASE_URL=https://mahalla-ovozi.uz` to `deploy/compose/.env.example` so the value is configured rather than defaulted, and remove the literal from `telegram-watchdog.ts:64` so an unset variable fails loudly at boot instead of silently pointing at a hardcoded host. The same edit should address the wider template gap: `apps/backend/src` reads roughly two dozen distinct environment variables (35 `process.env.*` sites) and the template documents five, leaving the remainder to undocumented in-code defaults.
- Confidence: Confirmed for the read site, the template's omission and the watchdog's registration (all read on disk). Whether any real deployment runs under a hostname other than `mahalla-ovozi.uz` is UNKNOWN and needs the VPS.

##### NOT A DEFECT — dismissed, with the dismissing evidence

- **Item 4 — `context-snapshot.ts:15,71,101` `verbatimText` reaching provider logs or persisted `ai_operations.result_payload`. NOT A DEFECT.** All three cited lines were read and are accurate: the field is declared at `:15`, populated from the database at `:71` (`verbatimText: acceptedEvidence.verbatimText`) and `:101` (`verbatimText: row.verbatimText`), and interpolated into the prompt at `:204` (`| Text: "${item.verbatimText}"`). Carrying raw citizen text into the LLM prompt is the documented design, so the prompt path is not the question. Both exposure vectors the pointer names are closed on disk. **(a) Provider logs:** `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/ai-gateway.ts` contains **zero** `logger` and **zero** `console.` calls (grep returns no matches), and `apps/backend/src/adapters/ai-providers/http-provider-adapter.ts` likewise has no logging call — its only references to the prompt are the request-body construction (`:23`, `:39-41`, `:66-68`, `:109`, `:132-134`) and the `JSON.stringify(body)` at `:157` that sends it to the provider, which is the intended destination. The prompt is never written to a log. **(b) Persisted `result_payload`:** the write site at `apps/backend/src/modules/ai/jobs/semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:587` stores `resultPayload: aiResult.data` — the model's structured **output**, not the input prompt. The fast-fail write at `:285-291` stores exactly five keys, none of them input text. The gateway's contract makes the persisted shape caller-defined (`modules/ai/types.ts:70`, `schema: ZodType<T, any, any>`), and the output shape observed at `:285-291` contains only `is_relevant`, `relevant_lanes`, `exclusion_reason`, `accepted_message_ids` and `reasoning`. Independently, the read path enforces the invariant mechanically: `modules/ai/ai-operation-query-service.ts:44-54` lists `verbatimText` in `FORBIDDEN_PRIVACY_KEYS`, and `assertPrivacyBoundary` is called on all five read paths (`:115`, `:141`, `:153`, `:173`, `:186`), so any payload that did carry the key would be rejected rather than served. Raw text does enter `raw_payload` jsonb at `semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:301`, but that is the 14-day debug-retention path owned by H-12/L-22 and by L-40, not `result_payload`. **Residual, recorded not as a defect:** a privacy violation on the read path raises `PrivacyBoundaryViolationError` with `statusCode = 500` (`ai-operation-query-service.ts:37`), so it surfaces as a generic server error rather than a distinct alertable code — that item is already routed to Layer 5 (5.1) at the pointer for `ai-operation-query-service.ts:35-42` and is not 6.2's.
- **Items 7, 8, 11, 13 — the four ADR-reconciliation items tagged `6.1-6.2`. SUPERSEDED — ALREADY OWNED.** Each was adjudicated in Phase 6.1 and none is a config, env or timezone defect, so no 6.2 work remains. Item 7 (ADR 0002 non-transactional enqueue) and item 8 (ADR 0004 stale-rejection promise) are owned by **H-8** and **H-10**; item 11 (ADR 0002 null-on-conflict cause) is owned by **M-40**; item 13 (ADR tombstone expiry) is owned by **M-30**. Phase 6.1 verified that ADR 0002 (`docs/adr/0002-postgresql-pgboss-transactional-intake.md`, 19 lines) and ADR 0004 (`0004-optimistic-ai-concurrency-stale-rejection.md`, 19 lines) contain no normative rule text a config value could contradict, which is why the 6.2 half of each tag has no independent subject.
- **Item 9 — `adr/0010` retry-lifecycle mismatch. SUPERSEDED — ALREADY OWNED by H-11; its ADR-text half was raised as OQ-52 in Phase 6.1.** The ADR-scoped question is semantic, not config. The config-adjacent part of the retry path (`modules/issues/retry-service.ts:166-172` — `retryLimit: 3`, `retryDelay: 5`, `retryBackoff: true`, `singletonSeconds: 300`) is dispatch behaviour already covered there.
- **Item 14 — `worker.ts` unscrubbed `AiGatewayError.message`. SUPERSEDED — ALREADY OWNED by M-53, and not a `worker.ts` defect.** Phase 6.1 established by grep that `entrypoints/worker.ts` contains no `AiGatewayError` reference and no `.message` read, and that the real defect is at `modules/ai/jobs/semantic-relevance-job-handler.ts:743`. This phase found no logging defect in `worker.ts` either.
- **Item 16 — `register-webhook.ts:9,16` ngrok default and hardcoded bot id. SUPERSEDED — ALREADY OWNED by F-14.** Re-verified at the exact cited lines in Phase 6.1; F-14's fix text already covers both literals.
- **`deploy/compose/.env.example:11-14` naming `AI_PROVIDER=GROQ`. SUPERSEDED — ALREADY OWNED by M-49.** Re-read this phase: `:12-14` still read `AI_PROVIDER=GROQ`, `AI_MODEL_ID=llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, `GROQ_API_KEY=replace_with_groq_api_key`, and `packages/api-contracts/src/analysis-settings.ts:4-9` still admits only `OPENAI`, `GEMINI`, `DEEPINFRA` and `OLLAMA`. M-49 owns this including its 2026-09-30 mechanism correction. Nothing in 6.2 changes it.
- **`APP_ORIGIN` fallback to a localhost literal when unset. SUPERSEDED — ALREADY OWNED by F-13.** `entrypoints/http.ts:134` and `modules/auth/origin-guard.ts:73` were re-read; the fail-open default is exactly as F-13 describes.
- **Timezone correctness against ADR 0003. VERIFIED SOUND — no defect.** ADR 0003 (`docs/adr/0003-same-day-calendar-boundary.md`, 19 lines) binds topics to a single Asia/Tashkent calendar day (`:8`). The implementation honours it without depending on the host clock: `packages/api-contracts/src/timezone.ts:8` states the design intent as pure UTC+5 arithmetic and `:21-25` applies it using only `getUTC*` accessors; `modules/telegram-intake/timezone-util.ts:26-28` derives both day boundaries as UTC instants shifted by the same constant. No host-timezone dependency exists anywhere: a grep of `apps/backend/src` for `getHours`, `getMinutes`, `getDate()`, `toLocaleDateString` and `toLocaleString` returns **no matches**, and a grep of the root `Dockerfile` for `TZ` returns no matches, so the container sets no timezone and none is needed. Uzbekistan has observed a fixed UTC+5 offset without daylight saving since 1992, so a constant offset is the correct model rather than a simplification.
- **Boot-time `NODE_ENV`. VERIFIED SOUND for the deployed image.** `Dockerfile:38` sets `ENV NODE_ENV=production`, and all three application services in `deploy/compose/docker-compose.prod.yml` additionally set `NODE_ENV: production` (`:33` backend, `:71` worker, `:101` userbot). The image default makes the value reliable even for an ad-hoc process started inside the container without the compose environment. This closes, for the containerised path, the residual M-50 recorded as UNKNOWN at its own entry ("whether production deployments always set `NODE_ENV=production`"); it does not resolve it for a process started outside the image.
- **`SESSION_COOKIE_NAME` and `APP_ORIGIN` effective production values. UNVERIFIABLE — already tracked as OQ-2.** `deploy/compose/.env.example:8-9` suggests `https://mahalla-ovozi.uz` and `mahalla_session`, and `modules/auth/session-manager.ts:9` defaults the cookie name to `mahalla_session`. The actual production values require the VPS env file, which is OQ-2's existing scope and is not duplicated here.

#### Phase 6.3 — Persisted `lastError` secret-leakage check

> **Execution record, added 2026-10-01.** The stub of the same name above remains as the record that no execution section existed before this date. This section is the executed phase.

Phase verdict: **verified sound — no defect found.** The pointer's specific concern — that a persisted `lastError` column could receive a Telegram API error containing a token-bearing URL — **does not hold**, because no code path anywhere derives `lastError` from a caught error object. Every value that can reach the column is a hardcoded literal or `null`. The phase therefore yields **ZERO findings** and consumes **no new finding IDs**.

**Counts: 0 Critical, 0 High, 0 Medium, 0 Low.**

**Method.** Read-only. One agent. No build, no typecheck, no test run, no database access, no git write. Every path was re-derived with grep; no line number was copied from this document. Read in full: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-groups/telegram-test-session-store.ts` (164 lines), `packages/api-contracts/src/telegram-groups.ts` (138). Read in cited ranges: `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-groups/telegram-groups-service.ts`, `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-groups/telegram-group-engine.ts`, `apps/backend/src/modules/telegram-groups/telegram-groups-routes.ts`, `apps/backend/src/adapters/db/schema/district-telegram-groups.ts`. Every write site was then followed to its origin, and every read site to its HTTP response.

##### What actually reaches `lastError` — the four sites traced to origin

**First correction to the pointer: two of its four cited sites are not writes.** `telegram-groups-service.ts:137` (`lastError: row.lastError || null`) is a **read** — it maps the DB column into the `TelegramGroupMapping` DTO inside `formatTelegramGroup`. `telegram-group-engine.ts:170` is also a read of the in-memory session, returned directly as a response field. Only `telegram-group-engine.ts:162` writes the column, and `telegram-groups-service.ts:519` writes `null`.

Tracing the only non-null writer, `telegram-group-engine.ts:162` reads `session.lastError || 'Синов вақти тугади.'`. The session store (`telegram-test-session-store.ts`) is the sole source, and it has exactly four assignments to that field:

- `:94` and `:113` — the literal `'Синов вақти (60 сония) тугади. Ҳақиқий одам томонидан хабар юборилмади.'` (the 60-second test timeout)
- `:131` — `undefined` (success path)
- `:143` — `session.lastError = errorReason`, the parameter of `resolveSessionFailure`

That last one is the only parameterised path, so it decides the question. `resolveSessionFailure` has **exactly one caller in the entire repository** — `telegram-groups-service.ts:612-616`, inside `deleteDistrictTelegramGroup` — and it passes a hardcoded domain literal:

```js
(options.sessionManager ?? globalTestSessionManager).resolveSessionFailure(
  districtId,
  groupId,
  'Гуруҳ ўчирилди',
);
```

**Therefore the set of values that can be _sourced_ into the persisted column is four items, all hardcoded:** `null`; the 60-second timeout message; the shorter `'Синов вақти тугади.'` fallback; and `'Гуруҳ ўчирилди'` (\"the group was deleted\"). No error object, no `err.message` read, no Telegram API response body and no URL-bearing string is ever the source. **CORRECTED 2026-10-01 by independent verification — the fourth literal is over-included in the set as originally phrased.** `'Гуруҳ ўчирилди'` provably cannot reach the column, for two independent reasons, both confirmed by reading the lines: (1) the write at `telegram-group-engine.ts:162` is guarded by `if (session?.status === 'TIMEOUT' && group.status === 'TESTING')` at `:157`, but `resolveSessionFailure` sets `session.status = 'FAILED'` at `telegram-test-session-store.ts:142`, so that branch is never entered on the delete path; (2) the same caller deletes the group row outright in a transaction at `telegram-groups-service.ts:618-622`, so there is no row left to carry the value even if the branch were reached. **The true value set that actually lands in the column is therefore three items:** `null`, the 60-second timeout message, and the `'Синов вақти тугади.'` fallback. The four-item list is retained above as a record of what the source analysis enumerated, and the corrected three-item set is the accurate statement. The verdict is unaffected — the removed item was a domain literal, never a credential-bearing value.

**Additional DB write sites — ADDED 2026-10-01 by independent verification, completing the enumeration.** Six further sites write the column, all of which assign the literal `null` and therefore strengthen rather than weaken the verdict: `telegram-group-engine.ts:128`, `:229`, `:300`; `telegram-bot/telegram-bot-service.ts:192`, `:330`; and `subscriptions-service.ts:928`. Each was read on disk and confirmed to be `lastError: null`. With the two sites already enumerated (`telegram-group-engine.ts:162` and `telegram-groups-service.ts:519`), the column has **eight DB write sites, of which exactly one can write a non-null value** — and that one writes one of the two hardcoded timeout literals.

**No scrubber is involved, and none is needed** — recorded because the pointer asked for it by name. There is no scrubbing layer on this path because there is no variable text to scrub. (For completeness: the codebase does own a token-URL redactor for the paths that genuinely handle tokens — `adapters/telegram/telegram-client.ts:112` with `redactTokenFromUrl:36-42` — but no Telegram error reaches `lastError`, so that machinery is simply not implicated here.)

##### Where the value is read and surfaced

Two read paths exist, and both were followed to the HTTP response. **[CORRECTED 2026-10-01 by independent verification — the enumeration below was materially incomplete as originally written.]**

The original text asserted \"Two read paths exist\" and named only the two below. That is wrong: `formatTelegramGroup` reads the field at `telegram-groups-service.ts:137` and is called from **five** call sites, not one — `:163` (`listDistrictTelegramGroups`, `rows.map`), `:196` (`getDistrictTelegramGroup`), `:359` (`createDistrictTelegramGroup`), `:401` (the update no-op early return), and `:568` (`updateDistrictTelegramGroup`). All five were re-verified on disk by grep this run (grep returns six matches: the definition at `:123` plus these five call sites). The field therefore surfaces on **four additional routes** beyond the two originally named — `GET /api/v1/districts/:districtId/groups/:groupId` (`routes:169`), `POST /api/v1/districts/:districtId/groups` (`routes:202`, status 201), and `PUT /api/v1/districts/:districtId/groups/:groupId` (`routes:236`) — all inside the same `createRequireProductOwner` scope (`routes:141-143`). **There is no privilege-escalation consequence:** every surface is behind the identical Product Owner guard, so the verdict is unchanged. The enumeration is corrected because the document must not carry a wrong count as fact. (Note for precision: the verifier described this as \"SIX sites\"; the verified count is five call sites plus one definition. The five call sites are what is stated here.)

- `telegram-groups-service.ts:137` → reached via `formatTelegramGroup`, called from **five** sites: `listDistrictTelegramGroups` (`:163`), `getDistrictTelegramGroup` (`:196`), `createDistrictTelegramGroup` (`:359`), the update no-op early return (`:401`), and `updateDistrictTelegramGroup` (`:568`). Surfaces on `telegram-groups-routes.ts:151-152` (`GET /api/v1/districts/:districtId/groups`, sent as `reply.status(200).send({ groups })`), `:169` (`GET .../groups/:groupId`), `:202` (`POST .../groups`, status 201), and `:236` (`PUT .../groups/:groupId`).
- `telegram-group-engine.ts:170` and `:185` → returned by `getGroupTestStatus` → `telegram-groups-routes.ts:292-299`, `GET /api/v1/districts/:districtId/groups/:groupId/test-status`, sent as `reply.status(200).send(result)`.

Both are declared in the contract — `packages/api-contracts/src/telegram-groups.ts:23` (`TelegramGroupMappingSchema.lastError`) and `:124` (`GetGroupTestStatusResponseSchema.lastError`), each `z.string().nullable()`. **Every** route that surfaces the field — all six call sites listed above, not only the two originally cited — sits inside the scope that registers `createRequireProductOwner` (`telegram-groups-routes.ts:141-143`), so the value is exposed only to an authenticated Product Owner. No log line writes the field: a grep for `lastError` across `apps/backend/src` returns the sites enumerated above and no logging call.

**Consumer check — ADDED 2026-10-01.** A grep of `apps/web/src` for `lastError` returns **zero matches**: the field is never consumed by the UI. The client-side exposure surface is therefore the API response only. Consistent with M-45, and noted here so a later reader need not re-derive it.

**NOTED OBSERVATION — not a finding, no ID allocated — `telegram-group-engine.ts:229` sits on a public unauthenticated path.** Added 2026-10-01 by independent verification and re-confirmed on disk. The write at `:229` is one of the six confirmed `lastError: null` sites recorded above, so it leaks nothing today. What is worth recording is its **reachability**: it sits inside `handleIncomingWebhookMessage`, entered from `telegram-groups-routes.ts:82` — `POST /api/v1/telegram/webhook/:botId`, registered on the root instance *outside* the `createRequireProductOwner` scope, guarded only by the HMAC-derived secret-token `preHandler` at `:86-102`. That is the documented public intake ingress whose HMAC verification Phase 1.1 recorded as sound. The observation is that an attacker-influenced value would have a path to this column **if** a future change made that handler write something other than `null`. Today it writes `null`, so there is no defect and no ID is allocated. It is recorded rather than dismissed because the guard on this particular write site is a constant, not a type, and it is the only one of the eight write sites reachable without authentication.

**Two benign additional reads — ADDED 2026-10-01.** `district-onboarding-engine.ts:237-240` and `health-checker.ts:639-642` both `select()` the full `districtTelegramGroups` row but never reference the `lastError` field. Neither is a value read path; recorded only so the next enumeration is complete.

**Residual, recorded not as a finding.** Neither route declares a Fastify `response` schema — consistent with the structural gap already recorded as **M-45** (the only five `response` schemas in the backend are in `health-routes.ts`). That is M-45's subject and consumes no ID here. Separately, the `errorReason: string` parameter of `resolveSessionFailure` is shaped to accept an arbitrary string, so a *future* caller could pass a message body. It is not a defect today — the parameter is domain-named and its single caller passes a domain literal — and manufacturing a Low for a hypothetical future caller would be exactly the invented finding this phase was instructed not to produce. It is noted here so the next reader knows the invariant is upheld by caller discipline rather than by the type, and a value object or union would harden it if that path ever gains a second caller.

##### ADJUDICATION — item 6: **NOT A DEFECT**

The pointer's stated risk — \"verify it cannot receive a Telegram API error containing a token-bearing URL\" (`telegram-groups-service.ts:137,519`, `telegram-group-engine.ts:162,170`) — is **refuted**. The column cannot receive such a value because no code path connects a caught error to it. The premise of the check does not survive contact with the code.

This is a legitimate zero-finding phase, on the same footing as Phase 4.3. It is recorded explicitly rather than silently, because a pointer that asserts a risk and finds none is a result worth stating: the risk was tested and is absent.

**IDs consumed by this phase: none.** No Critical, High, Medium or Low was raised, and no Open Question. The authoritative next-free set is unchanged at **`C-5 / H-26 / M-54 / L-43, OQ-53`**.

No `6.4` row exists or is needed. **Everything in Phase 6 is executed.** One item remains partially unresolved and is named rather than hidden: the runbook half of **item 10** is access-blocked — it needs the production runbook set plus `/opt/mahalla-ovozi` on the VPS, because `deploy/backup/` does not exist in this repository (M-47) and no reachable runbook under `deploy/` names the `cli:reconcile-retention` entrypoint. ## ADR-Accepted Risks — Re-confirm or Revoke

**AR-1. No row-level security; tenant isolation is entirely application-enforced.** Source: ADR 0006. Consequence: a single missing `district_id` filter is a cross-tenant data leak with no database backstop. **Status: re-confirmed as a live trade-off — Phase 1.3 found no actual cross-tenant leak**, so the bet is currently being honoured. But the audit produced six structural Medium findings (M-7…M-12) showing the only remaining backstop is discipline in the route layer, and half the service signatures accept a bare `districtId` string rather than a scoped actor. Recommendation: keep ADR 0006, but adopt M-11/M-12's fix (required district scope / `ALL_DISTRICTS` sentinel) so omission fails to compile instead of failing open.

**AR-2. Userbot transport risks (unofficial Telegram client automation) accepted.** Source: ADR 0009. Consequence: account bans and transport instability are accepted, not defects. **Status: RE-CONFIRMED by Phase 3.2, with weakened containment claims.** No new transport risk was found — bans, `FLOOD_WAIT` and instability remain inherent to MTProto and are correctly accepted. The unified-ingestion-core and passive-only-port architecture holds. **Caveat:** the ADR justifies accepting the ban risk partly on its stated containment ("automated ban detection transitions session status to BANNED", "kill switch … halt MTProto consumption instantly", ADR 0009:28,30). H-16 shows the kill switch can be silently reverted by an in-flight bootstrap, and M-35 shows an exported setter that can move BANNED → ACTIVE unguarded. The acceptance stands, but fixing H-16 and M-35 should be treated as preserving its validity rather than as new scope. **FURTHER WEAKENED by Phase 3.4:** H-22 shows GramJS is not a declared, locked or installed dependency (`apps/backend/package.json:26-47` has no `telegram` entry), so in the deployed image **no MTProto connection is ever made**. Both containment mechanisms named in ADR 0009:28 and :30 — automated ban detection transitioning the session to `BANNED`, and abnormal-signal alerts — therefore **cannot fire at all**, because there is no connection whose failure they could detect. The ban risk itself remains inherent and correctly accepted; but the ADR's containment narrative is now unreachable in practice, and the `userbot` service fails silently (session stays `ACTIVE` with `last_seen_at` frozen) rather than reporting the condition. Fixing H-22, H-16 and M-35 should all be treated as preserving the validity of this acceptance rather than as new scope. Owner for the ADR text correction: Layer 6 (6.1).

> **CONTESTED PREMISE — REVERSED 2026-10-01: THIS NOTE IS ITSELF FALSE. Read this correction before the note below.** The note's sole factual premise — that `apps\backend\package.json:38` reads `"telegram": "2.26.22"` — is contradicted by the file. Confirmed by direct read in this run: the file is **48 lines**; **line 38 reads `"zod": "catalog:"`**; and a grep of the whole file for `telegram` returns **zero matches**. The declared dependencies occupy `:26-39` and the devDependencies `:40-47` — exactly the ranges the note cites — and neither contains a `telegram` entry. **Consequence: H-22's original evidence stands as correct, and the AR-2 containment narrative above is NOT contested.** The mechanisms named at ADR 0009:28 and `:30` genuinely cannot fire, because no MTProto connection is ever established. The status header at line 3 and H-22's own `RESOLVED.` note both already carry the correct 2026-10-01 finding (zero `telegram` matches); this note was the sole dissent, and it was wrong. Its secondary inference — that H-22's later `RESOLVED.` annotation is "consistent with `package.json:38`" — is void for the same reason. **This is the first false state-claim found in the correction layer rather than in the original audit:** a note written to repair the record introduced an error of exactly the kind it existed to catch, and it was falsifiable by one read of a 48-line file. It is left unaltered below per this document's append-only convention — read it as a record of a mistake, not as a live claim. The same false premise had propagated to the Phase 4.2 bullet (corrected), the pre-execution 6.1 stub (corrected), and rows 2 and 12 of the Layer 6 reconciliation table (corrected).
>
> **CONTESTED PREMISE — recorded 2026-09-30 (AR-2 / H-22 contradiction).** The paragraph above rests on H-22's stated basis that GramJS "is not a declared, locked or installed dependency (`apps/backend/package.json:26-47` has no `telegram` entry)". Disk contradicts that basis as written: `C:\codevision-works\mahalla-ovozi\apps\backend\package.json:38` reads `"telegram": "2.26.22"`. The dependency **is** declared, at the exact path H-22 cites as containing no such entry — H-22's own evidence line (`:26-39` for dependencies, `:40-47` for devDependencies) excludes line 38 from neither range; the entry sits inside the range H-22 names. Consequently the AR-2 "unreachable" premise **does not hold as stated**: it is not established that no MTProto connection is ever made, because the manifest half of H-22's two-part claim is contradicted by the file. What remains standing, and what this note does not disturb: H-22's later `**RESOLVED.**` annotation at its own entry records the dependency as declared and installed at `2.26.22` and both `StringSession` and `TelegramClient` as resolving — consistent with `package.json:38`. The unresolved half is OQ-47 (whether the production host or image installs GramJS out-of-band), which stays open and is not answered either way by the declaration. **Disposition: the AR-2 containment narrative is therefore CONTESTED, not confirmed-unreachable.** H-22's original text above is left unaltered per the append-only convention; this note records the contradiction rather than resolving it, because the audit trail must carry both the original claim and the on-disk counter-evidence. Owner for the ADR text correction remains Layer 6 (6.1).

Pointers raised by Phase 4.1, routed to their owning layers:

- **Layer 5 (5.1, observability).** `deploy/compose/Caddyfile` declares no `log` directive and no access-log output path, while `entrypoints/http.ts:93-101` leaves Fastify's `disableRequestLogging` at its `false` default. The only per-request record that exists is Fastify's stdout line inside the backend container. Incident response has no edge-side request log to correlate against — no client IP, no status code, no user agent for any request Caddy rejects before proxying.
- **Layer 5 (5.1, observability).** No timeout is configured at either layer — Fastify's `connectionTimeout`, `requestTimeout` and `handlerTimeout` are all explicitly `0` and the Caddyfile sets none. Slow-request occupancy is therefore invisible to every current probe: `/api/v1/health/live` answers as long as the event loop turns, and `/api/v1/health/ready` probes only the database, the queue and restore reconciliation. H-23 would not surface as a health failure until the sockets were already exhausted.
- **Layer 6 (6.1–6.2, contract reconciliation).** `packages/api-contracts` defines response types for many endpoints, but not one is wired as a Fastify `response` schema — the only five response schemas in the backend are hand-written in `modules/health/health-routes.ts`, and three of them cover public probes. The contract layer and the wire layer have no mechanical link, so a contract can change without the serialization changing, and vice versa. M-45 is the symptom; the missing link is the structural cause.
- **Layer 4 (4.2, container & deploy correctness).** `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` is a file that lives under `deploy/` yet publishes PostgreSQL on 5433 with a default password and sets `NODE_ENV: development` on its `userbot` service. **RESOLVED by Phase 4.2 — every element confirmed, and the open sub-question answered: nothing points at this file.** `deploy/README.md` names no compose file at any line, and the two operator scripts (`package.json:17-18`) use bare `docker compose` from `/opt/mahalla-ovozi`, which resolves the **root** `docker-compose.yml`, not this one — the same bare form the userbot runbook uses (`deploy/runbooks/userbot-account-warmup.md:75`). Phase 4.2 allocates no new finding here: the content is already F-12 (default password), L-34 (published port and root duplicate) and C-1 (the encryption-key condition). The bare invocations are their own defect and are recorded as **L-36**; the C-1/OQ-5/OQ-6 dependency this pointer names is unchanged.]** Whether any runbook, README or muscle-memory invocation points at it decides whether C-1's fallback encryption key is reachable in a deployed context — the same question OQ-5 and OQ-6 raise for the production env file, answerable here from the repository alone.
- **Layer 4 (4.3, observability).** Every 5xx the client receives is the fixed string `INTERNAL_ERROR_MESSAGE` with a code that may be an arbitrary pass-through string (`api-error-envelope.ts:122-136`). There is no server-side classification of *why* the 500 occurred that a client, a probe or a dashboard can act on — the detail exists only in the Pino log line at `entrypoints/http.ts:153-161`.

1. Whether production sets `NODE_ENV=production` and `ENCRYPTION_KEY` (OQ-5, OQ-6, OQ-36). Needs read access to the VPS env file.
2. Whether a WAF, load balancer or host firewall rate-limits in front of Caddy (OQ-3, OQ-39, OQ-48). Needs VPS network and firewall configuration.

## Open Questions

- **OQ-1 (product decision).** Is the unbounded DISTRICT_HOKIM session renewal a deliberate wall-display requirement, or an unintended consequence? Determines whether H-3's fix is a hard cap or a documented exception.
- **OQ-2.** Effective production values of `SESSION_COOKIE_NAME` and `APP_ORIGIN` are unverified (no remote access permitted). `.env.example:8-9` suggests `mahalla_session` and `https://mahalla-ovozi.uz`.
- **OQ-3.** Whether an nginx/firewall layer sits in front of Caddy on the VPS is not determinable from the repo; `docker-compose.prod.yml:117-119` publishes only 80/443 from Caddy.
- **OQ-4.** Whether any runbook expects district suspension to revoke sessions; ADR 0007:18 asserts DB invalidation the code does not implement.
- **OQ-5.** Whether any deployment sets `NODE_ENV` to non-`production` while `ENCRYPTION_KEY` is unset — the only route to the dev fallback key (see C-1).
- **OQ-6 (blocks C-1 severity).** Whether the production `.env` actually defines `ENCRYPTION_KEY`, and whether every process (http, worker, userbot, and any ad-hoc `node`/CLI invocation inside the container) inherits it. C-1 is only exploitable if some process resolves the key without it — the fallback is otherwise unreachable. Resolving this requires reading the VPS env file, which is out of scope for this audit.
- **OQ-7.** Whether the web client persists or forwards the Hokim create/reset response body (F-5) to any logging or analytics sink. Not investigated — a web-layer question.
- ~~**OQ-8.** Whether `sanitizeDriverError` should be applied in the **global** error handler; today it is applied only on the telegram-intake path (`webhook-security.ts:44-64`). Ties to F-8/F-15.~~ **CLOSED by Phase 4.1** — RESOLVED: **no sanitizer is needed for message text.** `resolveErrorMessage` (`api-error-envelope.ts:130-136`) returns the fixed `INTERNAL_ERROR_MESSAGE` for every `statusCode >= 500`, unconditionally, so raw driver text is already unreachable through the global handler. The residual gap is the **`code`** field, not the message: `resolveErrorCode` (`:122-128`) forwards `error.code` verbatim on every status with no 5xx carve-out, so a `pg` error's `23505` or `42P01` reaches the client as the envelope `code`. That is already recorded as **F-15**; the `details`/`blockers` half is **F-8**. OQ-8's reference to "F-8/F-15" is therefore satisfied by those two existing entries and this phase allocates no F ID.
- ~~**OQ-9.** Does `district-deletion-service` run inside a single transaction?~~ **RESOLVED — YES.** `district-deletion-service.ts:158` opens one `db.transaction`, takes `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` at `:162-167`, and runs `tx.delete(districts)` (`:306`), `recordAuditEvent(tx, …)` (`:309-324`) and the tombstone read (`:326-330`) on the same `tx` handle before it closes at `:333`. A rollback cannot orphan the deletion record. (Verified on disk; severity adjudication belongs to Phase 2.2.)
- **OQ-10.** Does any writer outside the zod-validated API touch `accounts.username` or `district_subscriptions.internal_note`? M-17 assumes the API is the only writer; `seeds.ts` and `cli/` were not read. If they write directly, M-17's practical severity rises.
- **OQ-11.** Does the AI gateway validate model output against `QualifyingLaneSchema` before persisting? H-6's blast radius depends on it — the contracts were read, but not the validation boundary. → Owned by Phase 3.1.
- **OQ-12.** Live row counts / growth rate of `audit_events` and `user_dashboard_visits`. M-20 and L-5 are recommendations whose urgency scales with volume; no DB access was permitted.
- **OQ-13.** Are the nine missing snapshots deliberately deleted or never generated? A `.gitignore` check was not performed.
- **OQ-14.** Was the Phase 2.2 subagent report ever produced? **RESOLVED — NO** (first attempt). Transcript `384f1c85-7ed7-42f7-8a1a-8a45099bc8c9` ends at its task brief with zero assistant turns and zero tool calls, and `glob **/phase-*.md` finds no report file. The phase was re-run and **completed** (see Phase 2.2 findings above). Any assertion that `docs/phase-2.3-report.md` exists is false.
- **OQ-15 (blocks M-21/M-22/M-24 severity).** Are the four recovery schedules actually registered in production? The `pgboss.schedule` rows for `district-subscription-expiry-cron`, `district-live-deletion-cron`, `district-backup-expiry-cron` and `telegram-topic-projection-reconcile-cron` are created only when the corresponding `register*JobHandler` runs. The mitigation that holds M-21/M-22/M-24 at Medium rests on those sweeps firing — established from code, not live state. **If they are not firing, all three escalate to High.** Requires DB access this audit does not have.
- **OQ-16 (bears on C-2 severity).** Is there any operator-facing recovery or replay path for the `telegram-burst-debounce` / `telegram-content-qualification` queues? Phase 2.2 found no cron, no reconciliation sweep and no replay endpoint for either; `topic-query-engine.ts:645-657` only detects a delay. If a manual replay procedure exists outside `apps/backend/src` (a runbook in `deploy/README.md`, a SQL procedure), C-2's severity drops. **`deploy/` was not searched.**
- ~~**OQ-17.** pg-boss transactional `send` semantics under the `db.executeSql` option (`boss-client.ts:239-251`).~~ **RESOLVED — YES, the contract holds** (Phase 3.3, from pinned source). `createJob` destructures the override as `db: wrapper` (`manager.js:337`) and uses it for its only statement (`const db = wrapper || this.db`, `:377`; single call `:378`). No statement escapes the supplied `executeSql`. A singleton conflict returns `null` via `rows.length !== 1` (`:380-386`), so `retry-service.ts:174-179` reads `null` correctly in form — but `null` has a second cause (see M-40).
- **OQ-18.** Whether Drizzle transaction handles expose `.transaction` (affects L-15). The `'transaction' in db` guards at `district-analysis-settings-service.ts:252`, `:407`, `:587` behave differently depending on the drizzle version's nested-transaction support; could not be confirmed without running code. L-15 is recorded as an unverified hypothesis.
- **OQ-19.** The observed collision rate for H-8 — how often two semantic-relevance jobs for the same district/mahalla/calendar-day overlap within their AI inference window. The code defect is established; token-cost and duplicate-work impact is unquantified.
- **OQ-20.** Whether the 5-minute sweep re-invokes `verifyDistrictBackupExpiry` indefinitely for a district whose verifier keeps returning "not expired" (branch 2, `district-deletion-service.ts:575-581`, returns without any write), i.e. a hot loop with no backoff. The sweep's own job options were not traced.
- **OQ-21 (blocks C-3 severity).** pg-boss `expireInMinutes` versus the worst-case handler duration. The AI handlers run with `expireInMinutes: 10` (`boss-client.ts:30-43`) and call an LLM through `HttpProviderAdapter`. Whether a single AI job can exceed 10 minutes and be re-delivered while still running could not be settled — the HTTP client's timeout was not read. If it can, that is at-least-once double-execution on the AI path (the CAS and unique indexes absorb it, at the cost of duplicate tokens).
- **OQ-22 (blocks C-3 severity).** Whether the live `pgboss.queue.policy` values match the code's intent. `createQueue` is idempotent and does **not** update the policy of an existing queue (`plans.js:257`, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`). If any of these queues were created before `policy: 'short'` was added to the projection queue, the live policy is whatever it was then. Cannot be settled without querying the database.
- ~~**OQ-23 (bears on M-27).** Whether this deployment's `pgboss.job` carries a legacy `createdon` column or a compatibility view.~~ **RESOLVED — NO such column exists; M-27's detector arm is dead code** (Phase 3.3, from pinned source, independently re-verified by the orchestrator). `plans.js:193` creates the column as `created_on`; a source-wide search of the pinned version finds no `createdon` column and no compatibility view (only the `queue_created_on` PL/pgSQL variable at `plans.js:231` / `migrationStore.js:78`). `topic-query-engine.ts:655` therefore throws `column "createdon" does not exist` on every call, swallowed by the bare `catch {}` at `:661-663`. M-27's comment attributing this to test environments is wrong — the arm is dead in every environment. **M-27 needs no downgrade; its dead-arm claim is now Confirmed rather than conditional.**
- ~~**OQ-24.** Whether `expireDistrictGrace` is transactionally guarded against two concurrent sweeps.~~ **RESOLVED — YES, it is safe** (Phase 3.3). `subscriptions-service.ts:558-609` opens a transaction, locks `districts` `FOR UPDATE` (`:565`) then `district_subscriptions` `FOR UPDATE` (`:579`), and returns early when the locked row is no longer `GRACE` (`:582-585`) or the deadline has not passed (`:587-593`). Overlapping sweeps serialise on the row lock and the loser no-ops, so the missing `singletonKey` is a non-issue.

The extension branch is gated by `timeSinceLatest < SLIDING_DEBOUNCE_WINDOW_SECONDS && totalElapsed <= MAX_DEBOUNCE_BURST_CEILING_SECONDS` (`burst-debounce-job-handler.ts:121-124`; constants 30 and 60 at `:32-33`), and `firstMessageTimestamp` is carried forward on every re-arm (`:140`), including the edit path (`telegram-intake-service.ts:484`). Each re-arm is a new INSERT (`manager.js:334-396` never updates a row in place), so `expireInMinutes` bounds the residue, not the chain.

- **OQ-44 (bounds H-20's window).** Does the deployment ever run more than one `worker` replica, or run the HTTP process with a queue-list filter (`worker.ts:54,86`, `shouldWork`)? If unfiltered, L-31 is a real recurring-cost item; if filtered, it is informational. **Note:** H-21 makes this moot until the missing verifier module lands.

**Forward statement — deferrals to be resolved before the affected Layer 4 fixes are applied (2026-10-01).** The seven deferrals recorded above (item 10's runbook half, OQ-46, OQ-47, OQ-48, OQ-49, OQ-50, OQ-51) are **DEFERRED — ACCESS-BLOCKED**, not outstanding. They do not block this audit's closure: every one of them is bounded to a named finding and every affected finding has an owner. They do block the *fixes*, and each one must be resolved before the fix it bounds is applied — OQ-49 before any Layer 4 fix (it decides which compose file the fix must target), OQ-46 before M-42's grading and fix, OQ-47 before H-22's transport fix is called complete, OQ-48 before M-43 and M-44 are closed as latent, OQ-50 before L-37's pin is removed, OQ-51 before M-50's verifier is treated as the only backup mechanism, and the runbook half of item 10 before the M-40 fix is declared finished. Resolving all seven requires one thing this audit did not have: access to the production host (`/opt/mahalla-ovozi`, the runbook set, the env file, and `pgbackrest info`).

## Remediation close-out — H-21, H-22, M-50 (2026-10-02)

The three findings adjudicated OPEN on 2026-10-01 have been remediated in code and independently verified. Their original entries above are retained unaltered per the append-only convention; the dated annotations appended to each entry record the new state. Where an entry's FINAL ADJUDICATION block still asserts OPEN, that assertion is superseded by this close-out.

**H-21 — CLOSED.** Two modules were reinstated: the backup verifier adapter and the test double the District backup-expiry suite imports alongside it. All four production construction sites now resolve, and the typecheck gate passes.

**H-22 — CLOSED.** The GramJS library is declared as `^2.26.22` in the backend manifest, present in the workspace lockfile, and linked into the backend package. Correcting one detail of the original entry: this finding never broke the typecheck. The library is imported through a computed specifier, so the compiler never attempted to resolve it. Its real failure mode was at runtime, where the unlinked package threw when the opt-in transport loaded.

**M-50 — CLOSED.** The fail-open branch is deleted outright. The environment no longer participates in the decision, the missing-binary path returns not-expired with the error in the top-level field the deletion service actually reads, and a new distinct verification-method value identifies the case in the Audit Record. A consumer trace through `district-deletion-service.ts` confirms the corrected outcome: an un-runnable check now yields FAILED, where it previously yielded VERIFIED and authorised District data release.

**Verification evidence:**
- Workspace typecheck (the exact CI invocation): exit 0.
- Frozen-lockfile install: exit 0.
- District backup-expiry integration suite: 10 of 10 passing against the isolated test database.
- New backup-verifier unit suite: 7 of 7 passing, including a case that drives the real external-command path with a nonexistent binary, proving the production path fails closed and not merely the injected-resolver seam.
- Userbot bootstrap suite: 7 of 7 passing.
- Runtime module resolution confirmed from the backend package for both the bare and subpath import specifiers.

**Recorded deviation, reviewed and accepted.** The reinstated adapter wraps the injected backup-information resolver in a `try/catch` that the reference copy did not have. A resolver failure carrying a missing-binary error now reports the new verification-method value instead of the reference's generic verification-error value. Both outcomes fail closed, so deletion safety is unaffected; the difference is observable only to an audit query that discriminates on verification method. Recorded here rather than left silent.

**Not performed.** The container image build was not executed; the frozen-lockfile gate it depends on passes, but image contents are inferred rather than observed. Nothing in this remediation has been committed to version control.

**Newly recorded items (2026-10-02).** Two items identified while preparing the H-21/H-22/M-50 remediation are recorded here without code change, per this file's append-only convention.

- **NEW — OPEN (2026-10-02). Over-broad missing-binary predicate (audit-accuracy only; not fixed).** In `apps/backend/src/adapters/backup/system-backup-verifier.ts` around line 83, the predicate that classifies a missing binary is `errMessage.includes('ENOENT') || errMessage.includes('not found')`. The bare `'not found'` match is over-broad: an `execFile` rejection whose stderr says something like `stanza not found` would be mislabelled as `PGBACKREST_CLI_NOT_FOUND` rather than taking the execution-failed path. **Both paths fail closed, so District deletion safety is unaffected — this is an audit-accuracy defect, not a safety defect.** Recorded here rather than fixed, to keep the current remediation commit to its reviewed change set.

- **NEW — RESOLVED BY ANNOTATION (2026-10-02). Case 7 vs the spec's no-external-binary invariant (no code change).** The added unit suite contains one case that drives the real `execFile` path with a nonexistent binary (`pgbackrest-definitely-not-installed-xyz`), while the remediation spec stated that no test in the repository executes an external binary. The case is retained deliberately: it is the only coverage of the M-50 defect site — every other case injects a `backupInfoResolver` and returns before the external-command path. The spec now carries a dated annotation explaining the tension; see `docs/specs/backup-retention-verification-remediation.md`.
